- [x] **Device factory reset procedure** — full SANITIZE + SHDR wipe + warranty token clear + new TRNG salt + new warranty token. Spec in NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 1 §B. **Firmware IMPLEMENTED + host-tested (2026-07-13):** `firmware/factory_reset/` — `np_factory_reset_execute()` runs the 12-step R-1..R-12 sequence (§B.3); `np_factory_reset_resume_after_powerloss()` re-runs the idempotent R-5..R-10 core on interrupted-reset detection (§B.4, SNVS_LPGPR1 bit 0). Fail-safe invariant: on ANY HAL step failure the sequence returns the error WITHOUT clearing `reset_in_progress`, so the next boot re-wipes and the device never comes up half-erased. New salt + warranty token generated via TRNG (§A.6); sensitive stack buffers `memset_explicit`-zeroed on every path. Bounded HAL retry (`NP_FR_HAL_MAX_RETRIES`=3) guards transient eMMC/TRNG faults without unbounded looping. **24 host tests** (`tests/np_factory_reset_tests.c`) — happy path + abort-safety (flag STAYS SET on each of R-5/R-6/R-7/R-8/R-9/R-10 failure) + bounded-retry (always-fail called exactly 3×) + retry recovery + resume-path invariant; host-only failure injection via `include/np_factory_reset_test_hal.h` (`np_fr_host_hal`, entirely `#ifdef NPTEST_HOST`). Builds clean under `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` on host AND Cortex-M7 (`nm` confirms no host symbols in the target object). Wired into root `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` (`add_subdirectory` + `add_test`) and CI `firmware-host-tests.yml`. **Residual (hardware, external):** target-side platform HAL `OI-RESET-01..05` (real eMMC SANITIZE/zero, TRNG, Config write) and `FAI-RESET-01` SANITIZE-timing bench on the i.MX RT1062 (per OI-EMMC2-03) before any device ships outside the company.
- [ ] **UHDR two-layer key scheme** — master key wrapper (UKMD + WKMD). Spec in NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 1 §C. BLOCKING for UHDR firmware implementation; supersedes single-layer Argon2id spec in NP-FW-EMMC-001 Rev 1 §6. Cryptography reviewer sign-off required (OI-EMMC2-02). **Firmware IMPLEMENTED + host-tested (2026-07-08):** `firmware/uhdr_key/` — `np_ukmd_record_t` (§C.3), `np_uhdr_key_provision` / `_unlock` (§C.4) / `_background` (§C.4 step 7) / `_change_credential` (§C.5 — re-wraps the 32-byte UKMD only, UHDR ciphertext untouched, no remount). WKMD is a function-local, never in the context struct or any header, `memset_explicit`-zeroed before every return; only the GCM ciphertext+tag reach Config. HAL boundary (OI-UHDRK-01..07): TRNG, Argon2id, AES-256-GCM wrap/unwrap, Config read/atomic-write, UHDR XTS mount — real CAAM/DCP + Argon2id + RNGB backing pending. Host stubs model Argon2id/hw_bind/GCM with `np_crypto` SHA-256 so wrong-credential rejection (`NP_UHDR_ERR_AUTH`) is a genuine probe. The Argon2 `version` is plumbed through the KDF HAL (`np_uhdr_hal_argon2id`) and the host TRNG is counter-seeded so nonce-uniqueness is exercised (code-review CR-2/CR-3). 13 host test functions, `ctest` 9/9 green; wired into `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` (host + cross) and `firmware-host-tests.yml`. **Privacy-review hardening (2026-07-08):** (F1) **hardware binding** — `WKMD = hw_bind(Argon2id(credential, salt), device-fused HUK)` via new HAL **OI-UHDRK-09** (CAAM/OTPMK on target), so an eMMC image can't be brute-forced offline (defeats the §C.6 PIN-fallback offline vector); (F2) the salt + KDF params are **authenticated as GCM AAD** and stored params are **validated against a floor** (`NP_UHDR_ARGON2ID_*_MIN`) → `NP_UHDR_ERR_PARAM` on rollback; (F3) **every unlock failure path scrubs the UKMD**. (F4) UKMD key-material (incl. per-device salt) added to the SHDR-schema CI prohibition (`ci/test_shdr_schema.py` KEYMAT-01, 7 checks green) and a fleet-export prohibition documented in `np_uhdr_key_types.h`. **OI-UHDRK-08:** §C.3 struct is self-inconsistent — fields sum to 152 B but the comment says "Pad to 192 bytes"; resolved to the stated 192 B (`reserved[84]`), pending spec-author confirmation. Remaining before checkbox close: real AES-GCM/XTS/Argon2id + CAAM-HUK HAL implementations, and **cryptography-reviewer sign-off (OI-EMMC2-02) — scope MUST cover F1 (hardware binding / offline-attack resistance) and F2 (key-wrap construction; consider AES-KW / AES-GCM-SIV vs the current AES-GCM+AAD)**.

- [x] **EDF+ patient header policy** — patient code = 16-char UHDR token; sex/birthdate/name = 'X'. Header validator in research anonymization pipeline. Spec in NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 1 §E. **Firmware IMPLEMENTED + host-tested (commit 81f5bf5, verified 2026-07-14):** `firmware/edf/` — `np_edf_write_header()` fills a byte-exact 256-byte EDF+ main header (packed `np_edf_header_t`, compile-time size-checked) per §E.2/§E.3: `local_patient_id` = `"NP"` + 14 lowercase-hex chars from UHDR token bytes 0–6 (16-char opaque patient code) + ` X` (sex) + ` X` (birthdate) + ` NeurOne_User` (fixed name); `local_recording_id` = `"Startdate <DD-MMM-YYYY> NeurOne X NeurOne_v<fw>"`; `startdate`/`starttime` from `session_ts`. Fully self-contained — file-local integer `gmtime`/hex/field-pad helpers, no libc string/time, no heap, fixed-width non-NUL-terminated fields (identical bytes on host + target). `np_edf_validate_privacy_header()` (§E.4) rejects any non-`'X'` sex/birthdate, any real patient name (`np_edf_header_contains_real_name()` — compliant only for empty or exactly `NeurOne_User`), and any code not prefixed `"NP"` → `NP_EDF_ERR_PRIVACY_HEADER`/`NP_EDF_ERR_REAL_NAME`; null-safe fail-closed. **7 host tests** (`tests/np_edf_tests.c`) — OI-EMMC2-05 100-headers-generate-and-validate loop + 3 validator rejection paths (sex/DOB/name) + `"NP"` prefix + 256-byte size + fw-version-in-recording-id; **0 failures**. Builds clean under `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` on host AND cross-compiles clean for Cortex-M7 (`arm-none-eabi-gcc` 14.2.1, `-mcpu=cortex-m7 -mfpu=fpv5-d16 -ffreestanding`). Wired into root `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` (`add_subdirectory(edf)` + `add_test(np_edf_tests)`) and CI `firmware-host-tests.yml` (`firmware/edf/**` trigger). **Anonymization-pipeline gate ADDED (2026-07-14):** `np_edf_anon_gate_filter()` (`np_edf_anon_gate.{h,c}`) implements §E.4 as the per-file, exclude-not-abort staging filter — validates each candidate EDF+ header with `np_edf_validate_privacy_header()`, marks each `included[i]` true/false, and on a violation fires an injected SHDR-fault callback (`NP_EDF_SHDR_EV_PRIVACY_HEADER_VIOLATION` = 0xE1, distinct from the CVNS 0xC1..0xC7 range) then CONTINUES — a bad header excludes one file, never aborts the extract (returns `NP_EDF_OK` even when files were excluded; asserted by tests). Deliberately NOT the anon pipeline's extract-level `validate` callback (which does k/l/DP thresholds and DOES abort) — routing a per-file header check there would kill the whole extract on one bad file. Decoupled from `hub_control`: the gate never calls the SHDR logger itself; the fault is delivered through the injected callback (mirrors the anon pipeline's dependency-injection pattern). **4 new host tests** (mixed pass/exclude with correct fault indices+code, all-pass, all-fail-does-not-abort, null/empty/NULL-fault_cb) → 11 EDF tests total, 0 failures; gate source cross-compiles clean for Cortex-M7 under `-Werror`. **Residual (integration, external):** (1) writer still has no caller — wires in at the first EEG session recorder (target-side, unwritten); (2) the study-extract runner that iterates the real staged EDF+ files, calls `np_edf_anon_gate_filter()`, and maps its fault callback onto `np_log_shdr_fault(NP_EDF_SHDR_EV_PRIVACY_HEADER_VIOLATION, ...)` is the remaining hub/anon integration seam (the gate primitive it needs is now done).

See NP-PRIV-001 HIGH-01, NP-PRIV-REM-001 STEP-34.
- [x] **Washington MHMD protections — applied to ALL users (universalized 2026-07-10)** — Per the same durable principle ([[privacy-strictest-globally]]), the MHMD *user protections* on SHDR behavioral data (consumable counts, session counts) apply to every user, not gated to Washington activations: (1) **no-sale** — NeurOne does not sell, lease, or trade SHDR behavioral data (or any user data), a universal commitment stated unconditionally in the privacy notice; (2) **standalone authorization** — the warranty-owner authorization for SHDR uploads is separate and distinct from HIPAA consent and is obtained from every warranty owner, not just Washington registrants. MHMD RCW 70.372 is cited as the WHY; the control is global. **Open — confirms scope, does NOT gate shipping the protection:** WA-qualified counsel analysis of whether SHDR session/consumable counts are "consumer health data" under RCW 70.372.010(2). See NP-PRIV-001 HIGH-02, NP-PRIV-REM-001 STEP-35.

- [x] **SHDR fleet DB schema freeze gate (OI-EMMC2-07)** — CI schema test implemented and passing (7/7 checks). `ci/test_shdr_schema.py` parses `ci/shdr/shdr_fleet_schema.sql` and enforces: ACCEL-01 no prohibited accelerometer columns (g-force, accel_[xyz], orientation_*, drop_count, drop_timestamp, impact_*, fall_*, rms_g/peak_g/average_g, raw_accel); ACCEL-02 no numeric column types in any `accel` table (only BOOLEAN accel-derived fields permitted); ACCEL-03 required booleans `drop_detected`/`maintenance_alert` present; PII-01 no personal-data columns; KEYMAT-01 no UHDR key-material columns (Finding 4); TIME-01 no wall-clock timestamp columns (no TIMESTAMP/TIMESTAMPTZ/TIME type anywhere); NOJOIN-01 no cross-DB warranty-registration references; TOKEN-01 `warranty_token` is opaque BYTEA. Wired into CI via `.github/workflows/shdr-schema-ci.yml` (triggers on changes to the schema or test; BLOCKING). The gate is now armed and green (8/8); the actual SHDR fleet DB schema *freeze* remains a downstream project milestone that this test guards. Spec in NP-FW-EMMC-002 §G.5. **Schema Rev 2 (2026-07-13) — timestamp minimization:** every per-row `TIMESTAMPTZ` wall clock removed and replaced with the largest granularity that still serves a real function — per-session/event tables carry a month-granular `ingest_month DATE` (retention/TTL only; row ordering was already an integer counter — `session_index`/`gap_index`/`*_at_*`/`*_count`); `devices.updated_at`→`last_seen_month DATE` (liveness+retention); `devices.created_at` removed (device age = `manufacture_date`; activation date lives only in the consented warranty_db, no-join); `consumable_counts.updated_at` removed (reminders are count-triggered — no function needed it). This deletes a per-row docking-time correlator keyed to `warranty_token` and aligns the schema with the §5.1 "no timestamps in SHDR" rule; TIME-01 makes it durable.

- [x] **FreeRTOS-Kernel V11.3.0 vendored + integrated — SW-02 RTOS (2026-07-11):** Closes the SW-02 "FreeRTOS listed as SOUP but absent from tree; cannot cross-compile" gap. **Vendored** byte-exact subset of FreeRTOS-Kernel V11.3.0 (from FreeRTOS-LTS `202604.00-LTS`) into `firmware/vendor/freertos/` (mirrors the Monocypher SOUP pattern): `kernel/` (tasks, list, queue, event_groups, timers, stream_buffer + all 21 headers), `portable/GCC/ARM_CM7/r0p1/` (shipped target port), `portable/MemMang/heap_4.c` (shipped heap), `portable/ThirdParty/GCC/Posix/` (**host-test-only**, never in the device image), MIT `LICENSE.md`, and `VERSION` SOUP record. `croutine.c` excluded (deprecated; `configUSE_CO_ROUTINES 0`); all other ~60 CPU ports excluded. All 34 kernel files sha256-verified byte-exact vs upstream. **First-party (not SOUP):** `firmware/hub_control/include/FreeRTOSConfig.h` (600 MHz, 1 ms tick, `configMAX_PRIORITIES 6`, heap_4 64 KiB, `configUSE_PORT_OPTIMISED_TASK_SELECTION` on target, `configCHECK_FOR_STACK_OVERFLOW=2` + `configUSE_MALLOC_FAILED_HOOK=1` — substantiates the NP-SW-001 §9.4 stack-overflow-detection claim — `configASSERT`→`np_freertos_assert_failed`, Cortex-M NVIC priorities with `configPRIO_BITS 4`, and `SVC_/PendSV_/SysTick_Handler` aliases; a single `NP_FREERTOS_POSIX_HOST` guard makes the same file serve target + host); `firmware/hub_control/src/np_hub_freertos_hooks.c` (the three hooks — fail-safe by halting the main processor, which stops the SPI heartbeat so the STM32G071 safety MCU independently cuts stimulation <50 ms). **Build wiring:** `np_freertos` static library builds for Cortex-M7 (`-mcpu=cortex-m7 -mfpu=fpv5-d16 -mfloat-abi=hard`); root `NP_PLATFORM_INCLUDE_DIRS` populated with kernel/port/config dirs; `np_hub_control` links `np_freertos`; hooks added to hub sources. **Verification:** `np_freertos_smoke_tests` compiles the real kernel + this config + hooks against the POSIX port and runs the scheduler (prioritised tasks + event group + `vTaskDelayUntil`, mirroring the hub's `NP_EV_SESSION_START` pattern) → PASS; registered in root CMake ctest and `firmware-host-tests.yml` (trigger path `firmware/vendor/**`); full host suite **10/10 green**. **NOT done (separate platform bring-up, not FreeRTOS-kernel work):** NXP MCUXpresso SDK (MIMXRT1062 CMSIS device header, clock init), startup/vector table, SRAM/LPSDR4 linker script, and the final linked application target; plus the hub platform-HAL `OI-*` stubs (SPI, transport, module drivers, eMMC log). On-target ARM_CM7 compile requires `arm-none-eabi-gcc` (not installed on the dev host). See `firmware/vendor/freertos/README-NEURONE.md`, NP-SW-001 §4.2/§9.4, NP-DHF-001 Rev 17.
- [x] **SW-01 Safety MCU firmware source authored + 10 critical bugs corrected (2026-06-08):** All 8 IEC 62304 Class C modules implemented: np_gpio_mgr (M01), np_spi_watchdog (M02), np_charge_monitor (M03), np_thermal_interlock (M04), np_cardiac_interlock (M05), np_impedance_check (M06), np_session_sig (M07), np_fault_latch (M08). 7-angle code review surfaced 10 confirmed bugs; all fixed: **(1) CRITICAL — watchdog dead path:** `np_spi_watchdog_check()` was never called; added extern + call every main-loop iteration. **(2) Session transition wiring:** `np_session_sig_reset()`, `np_charge_monitor_reset_session()`, `np_impedance_check_request()` now called on `session_active` 0→1 transition. **(3) Impedance poll wired:** `np_impedance_check_poll()` now called every main-loop iteration. **(4) CUTOFF bit cleared unconditionally:** `np_spi_watchdog_tick()` cleared `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_CUTOFF` on any valid heartbeat, masking active thermal/charge/cardiac faults from main processor — now only clears when no other interlock active. **(5) `s_cutoff_active` never reset:** After 30s lockout expiry, cardiac interlock was permanently disabled for the session — now reset when lockout expires. **(6) Lockout wrap at ~49 days:** Absolute-timestamp `>=` comparison wraps at `UINT32_MAX` — replaced with elapsed subtraction `(now - start) >= period`. **(7) First RR phantom interval:** `s_last_capture=0` produced a phantom RR on first beat — guarded with `s_first_beat_seen` flag. **(8) 5s observation window not implemented:** `NP_CARDIAC_OBS_MS` defined but never used — rolling baseline now refreshed every observation window. **(9) `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_CARDIAC` sticky:** Never cleared, blocking CVNS re-enable permanently — cleared in new `np_cardiac_interlock_reenable()` called by main when hub asserts `NP_SESSION_STATUS_CVNS_REENABLE`. **(10) CVNS re-enable protocol:** New `NP_SESSION_STATUS_CVNS_REENABLE` bit (bit 1 of `session_status` frame byte) allows hub to signal explicit re-enable after cardiac cutoff; triggers reenable + fresh impedance check. See `firmware/safety_mcu/`.
- [x] **SW-01 Safety MCU second-pass code review — 9 additional bugs corrected (2026-06-08):** 7-angle second-pass review of all 8 Class C modules surfaced 9 additional bugs (8 fixed in code; 3 documented as open items requiring design work — see OI-CHARGE-01, OI-CHARGE-02 below (OI-SW01-M07-02 subsequently CLOSED 2026-06-11)): **(1) `.fault_latch` linker placement bug:** `.fault_latch` section floated immediately after `.bss` mid-SRAM rather than at the top-of-SRAM address where `_fault_latch_start` symbol pointed — warm-reset persistence silently broken; fixed by adding `. = _fault_latch_start;` inside the section body (`startup/stm32g071_flash.ld`). **(2) `rr_to_bpm()` int16_t overflow:** RR intervals < 1831µs (noise spikes, contact bounce, > 32767 BPM) silently overflow int16_t, producing phantom cardiac cutoffs or missed cutoffs; now saturates to INT16_MAX (`np_cardiac_interlock.c`). **(3) `current_hr_bpm()` double-call ISR race:** function called twice per main-loop tick with no atomic guard — an R-peak ISR arriving between calls produces an inconsistent HR delta that could both miss a true event and false-trigger; now called once into a local variable and reused. **(4) Baseline refresh desensitization after cutoff:** `s_baseline_bpm` refreshed unconditionally while `s_cutoff_active`, adopting elevated post-event HR as the new baseline and desensitizing cardiac detection for any subsequent event in the same session; refresh now gated on `!s_cutoff_active`. **(5) `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_IMPEDANCE` permanent lock:** IMPEDANCE status bit set on a failed check but never cleared on successful recheck — any single impedance measurement failure would permanently disable the affected channel for the device's operational lifetime; fixed: bit now cleared when re-check passes (`np_impedance_check.c`). **(6) Fault latch overwrite on first loop after warm reset:** `np_fault_latch_commit()` executing on the first main-loop iteration after a warm reset overwrote the preserved latch data (specific fault slot + detailed status bits) with the generic `FAULT=0x01 / slot=0xFF` from freshly-zeroed `s_state` — destroying the diagnostic forensic record; guarded by new `s_prior_latch_reported` flag (`np_safety_main.c`). **(7) GPIO startup comment inversion:** comment stated "All stimulation GPIO is driven LOW (cutoff) at startup" — incorrect for active-LOW system (LOW = enabled, HIGH = disabled) — would mislead HAL implementer and cause bring-up error; corrected to "HIGH (disabled) at startup; LOW = enabled". **(8) Double-evaluated fault flags in `np_spi_watchdog_tick()`:** four-flag OR expression evaluated twice with no critical section — if any interlock migrates to ISR, a fault asserting between the two evaluations clears CUTOFF while simultaneously granting the full requested mask (unsafe state); extracted to single local `uint8_t active_faults`. **(9) Fault latch count saturation:** `np_fault_latch_commit()` incremented `s_latch.count` every main-loop iteration while FAULT bit set — UINT16_MAX (65535) reached in ~65 seconds, freezing the event counter and hiding all subsequent distinct fault events in SHDR audit trail; now change-gated: count increments only when status or fault_slot actually changes (`np_fault_latch.c`). See `firmware/safety_mcu/`.
- [x] **OI-SW01-M07-01 CLOSED: Session signature delivery mechanism — 102-byte SPI command frame (2026-06-16)** — Two-frame SPI protocol designed and wired. New `np_safety_sig_cmd_t` (102 bytes, magic `0xC0 0xDE`): 2-byte magic + 1-byte cmd type + 1 reserved + 32-byte SHA-256 hash + 64-byte Ed25519 sig + 2-byte additive checksum. HAL distinguishes from 8-byte heartbeat by NSS-delineated transfer length. `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_SIG_PENDING` (bit 7) added to `np_safety_status_t`: set by `np_session_sig_reset(state*)` on session 0→1 transition; cleared by `np_session_sig_verify()` on valid sig; persists (+ FAULT + CUTOFF, fault_slot=0xFD) on bad sig. `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_SIG_PENDING` added to `active_faults` mask in `np_spi_watchdog_tick()` — blocks `granted_mask` until hub delivers signature. Hub-side `np_safety_spi_send_session_sig(hash, sig)` builds and sends the 102-byte frame; must be called after first heartbeat with `session_active=1` and before first non-zero enable request. Sequencing: heartbeat(session_active=1, mask=0) → MCU sets SIG_PENDING → hub sends 102-byte cmd → MCU verifies → hub checks SIG_PENDING in next heartbeat reply → if cleared, sends heartbeat(mask=channels). Files modified: `np_safety_protocol.h`, `np_session_sig.c`, `np_spi_watchdog.c`, `np_safety_main.c`, `np_safety_spi.h/.c`, `np_hub_config.h`, `np_hub_types.h`. **38 host tests added** (`firmware/safety_mcu/tests/np_safety_spi_proto_tests.c`): frame sizes + field offsets, magic distinctness, checksum round-trip + corruption detection, SIG_PENDING lifecycle, watchdog grant gating, constant values, heartbeat unaffected. All 38 pass. `np_safety_spi_proto_tests` added to root `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` host test suite (now 6 test binaries). Session sig verification now has full safety effect on `granted_mask`.
- [x] **OI-CVNS-HUB-01 CLOSED: hub-side `NP_SESSION_STATUS_CVNS_REENABLE` logic (2026-07-08)** — New pure-C, host-tested three-gate state machine `firmware/hub_control/src/np_cvns_reenable.c` (+ `include/np_cvns_reenable.h`) asserts the re-enable bit ONLY when ALL three conditions hold: (1) `NP_CVNS_REENABLE_LOCKOUT_MS`=30000 elapsed since the hub OBSERVED `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_CARDIAC` in a heartbeat reply (the hub window ⊇ the MCU's own 30s lockout because hub observation lags MCU cutoff by ≤1 beat); (2) explicit app confirmation via `np_cvns_reenable_confirm()`; (3) a fresh hub-side impedance check (HAL `np_cvns_hal_impedance_start`/`_poll`, OI-CVNS-HUB-02) PASSED. States IDLE→LOCKOUT→AWAIT_CONFIRM→IMPEDANCE→ASSERTING; only ASSERTING emits the bit; impedance fail/timeout and a 2s assert-timeout all fail-closed to AWAIT_CONFIRM; any session-boundary or external CARDIAC clear resets to IDLE. **Single-writer concurrency design (Forge audit findings 1+2):** `confirm()` (transport task) only posts a one-shot intent flag; the higher-priority heartbeat task is the SOLE state mutator and consumes/re-validates the flag from AWAIT_CONFIRM — race-free without a lock, and a stale confirmation is discarded on every reset and new-cutoff rising edge. Wired into `task_safety_heartbeat` (`np_hub_control_main.c`): drives the machine and sets the bit BEFORE building each TX frame (Forge finding 3 — guarantees CVNS_REENABLE and ACTIVE are consistent in the same frame, never REENABLE=1 with ACTIVE=0 during teardown). Recoverable-cutoff policy: a cardiac-only fault status (CARDIAC set, none of `NP_CVNS_NONRECOVERABLE_FAULTS`) no longer aborts the session — the hub holds it so re-enable is possible. **Two blocking SPI bugs fixed:** (a) `np_safety_spi_heartbeat()` was casting the `np_session_state_t` enum directly into `session_status` (RUNNING=3 spuriously set CVNS_REENABLE, PAUSED=4 set GEOM_REQUIRED) — replaced with pure host-tested helper `np_safety_session_status_bits()` (ACTIVE for RUNNING/PAUSED/STOPPING); (b) `task_safety_heartbeat` used a stale 2-arg signature and echoed the granted mask — now calls the 4-arg form and transmits the REQUESTED mask via new `np_safety_spi_get_requested_mask()`. Defense-in-depth: safety MCU `np_safety_main.c` re-enable now additionally gates on `s_state.session_active`. Session runner resets the manager at session start. SHDR lifecycle logging via `np_log_shdr_fault()` with `NP_CVNS_SHDR_EV_*` flag codes and suppressed timestamp (no HR/RR biology). **76 host tests** (`firmware/hub_control/tests/np_cvns_reenable_tests.c`) wired into `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` + `hub_control/CMakeLists.txt` NP_BUILD_TESTS path; full host suite **8/8 binaries, 244 checks, 0 failures**. CI `firmware-host-tests.yml` trigger paths extended to `firmware/hub_control/**`, `firmware/safety_mcu/**`, `firmware/common/**`. **RESIDUAL — OI-CVNS-HUB-02** (hub-side impedance HAL driver) and **OI-CVNS-HUB-03** (app-transport wiring of `np_hub_cvns_reenable_confirm()`) are stubbed integration points. FAI-CV02 hardware bench timing still required at T2 prototype. OI-CVNS-HUB-01 in NP-FW-HUB-001.

- [x] **OI-CHARGE-02 CLOSED: HD-tDCS electrode geometry wired from hub session runner to safety MCU charge monitor (2026-07-07)** — The hub session runner (`np_session_runner.c`) scans the verified session descriptor at session start; for any `NP_MOD_HD_TDCS` command with a `RING_4X1`/`BILATERAL_4X1` montage (3.5mm Ag/AgCl electrodes) it sends the electrode AREA (96 milli-cm² = floor(0.0962cm²×1000), `NP_HD_SMALL_ELECTRODE_AREA_MCM2`) for the CLIN_STIM channel (index 13, `NP_SAFETY_CH_CLIN_STIM`) to the safety MCU via a new 34-byte command frame `np_safety_chan_limit_cmd_t` (magic 0xC0/0xDE, `cmd_type=NP_SAFETY_CMD_CHAN_LIMIT` 0x02; `firmware/common/include/np_spi_wire_types.h`). Delivered by `np_safety_spi_send_channel_limits()` (`hub_control/src/np_safety_spi.c`). The safety MCU main loop reads it AFTER the session-start reset block (`np_safety_main.c`) and calls the new `np_charge_monitor_set_channel_area_mcm2()`, which derives `limit_nc = NP_CHARGE_LIMIT_UC_CM2 (40) × area_mcm2` → 3840nC = **3.84µC** on CLIN_STIM (vs the 1000µC pad default). **Design invariant:** the hub transmits electrode AREA only — the 40µC/cm² density constant stays resident on the Class C safety MCU. Area is floored so the derived limit never exceeds the true 40µC/cm² ceiling. **Tests:** +3 charge-monitor cases (HD-tDCS trip at 3.84µC, 25cm²-via-area = 1000µC, other-channel isolation) and +3 SPI-proto cases (34-byte frame size/offsets, checksum round-trip + corruption); host suite 7/7 green. **RESIDUAL — OI-CHARGE-03 CLOSED (2026-07-07):** the fail-open hole is now closed by a fail-safe geometry gate (see next bullet). FAI bench with a calibrated current source still required at G2 prototype. OI-CHARGE-02 in NP-SW-001.
- [x] **OI-CHARGE-03 CLOSED: fail-safe electrode-geometry gate blocks CLIN_STIM until HD-tDCS area applied (2026-07-07)** — Closes the OI-CHARGE-02 fail-open residual: if the electrode-area command is lost/corrupt, CLIN_STIM would keep the permissive 1000µC pad default. New shared heartbeat bit `NP_SESSION_STATUS_GEOM_REQUIRED` (bit 2, `firmware/common/include/np_spi_wire_types.h`) is asserted by the hub every 200ms while an HD-tDCS ring/bilateral montage is in the session (`np_safety_spi_set_geom_required()` in `hub_control/src/np_safety_spi.c`; armed by the session runner in `np_session_runner.c` before any CLIN_STIM enable; cleared on abort/end). The safety MCU reads it into `np_safety_state_t.geom_required` and `np_charge_monitor_geom_gate()` clears `NP_SAFETY_EN_CLIN_STIM` from `granted_mask` whenever `geom_required && !geom_applied` — where `geom_applied` latches true only when a valid area command sets CLIN_STIM's limit (`np_charge_monitor_set_channel_area_mcm2()`), and resets each session. The gate runs among the interlocks **before** the charge-accumulate loop (a gated channel accrues no charge). **Fail-CLOSED:** a dropped area command keeps HD-tDCS disabled until a retry lands, rather than running at the wrong limit. **Scoped to CLIN_STIM only** — clinical tACS shares enable bit 13 but never sets GEOM_REQUIRED, so it is never blocked. **No new MCU status bit** (the 8-bit status byte is full); the block is observable via CLIN_STIM absent from granted_mask, and the hub needs no prompt since it already sends the area command. **Irreducible limit (documented):** a hub that never declares GEOM_REQUIRED for an HD-tDCS session still runs at the default — HD-tDCS and clinical tACS share enable bit 13, so full closure would need per-electrode enable lines (hardware). **Tests:** +5 charge-monitor gate cases (blocked-until-applied, granted-after-applied, tACS-unaffected, CLIN_STIM-only, re-arm-per-session) and +4 SPI-proto session-status-bit assertions; host suite 7/7 green. OI-CHARGE-03 in NP-SW-001.
- [x] **Fault latch extended SPI command privacy gate — RE-RESOLVED (2026-08-12), reversing the conditional suppression below.** The 2026-07-14 resolution (retained verbatim after this paragraph as the record of what was believed then) suppressed `tick_ms` to 0 **only** for `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_CARDIAC`. That is now understood to be a defect, not a protection. Because `count` was reported unconditionally by an *independent* helper, the observable pair `count > 0 && tick_ms == 0` identified a cardiac fault **with certainty and with no correlation work** — a one-bit oracle that is strictly worse than no redaction at all, since a bare relative SysTick value only becomes sensitive when joined to a session record SHDR does not hold, whereas the redaction *pattern* is self-interpreting. **General rule now binding: a redaction applied conditionally on a sensitive predicate leaks that predicate; the redaction must be unconditional, or the predicate must not be inferable from the pattern of redaction.** Two further findings established during the fix: (1) **the suppression never protected the cardiac predicate in the first place** — the hub already writes the cardiac cutoff to SHDR as a first-class named event (`NP_CVNS_SHDR_EV_CUTOFF` 0xC1 → `fault_log.fault_type = 'CVNS_HR_CUTOFF'`, `ci/shdr/shdr_fleet_schema.sql`), by the locked "safety interlock log → SHDR" rule; (2) **fault event timing was already classified UHDR unconditionally** — `fault_log` has no timing column and states "Precise fault event timing, where it exists at all, is UHDR under the user's key", and `np_log_shdr_fault()` discards its `session_ms` argument for *every* caller. **New resolution:** `tick_ms` is **not SHDR-reportable at all** (device-internal only); `status`, `slot` and `count` are reported unconditionally through ONE fixed-shape marshaller `np_fault_latch_build_report()` → `np_fault_latch_report_t {status, slot, count}`. The retired per-field accessors `np_fault_latch_report_tick_ms()` / `_report_count()` are deleted — the oracle existed *because* they were independent, so the fix is structural (a fixed struct shape cannot have a field present for one fault kind and absent for another) rather than a different constant. `tick_ms` remains in the SRAM latch for MCU-internal timing. **23 host assertions** (`np_fault_latch_privacy_tests.c`, was 18), including a falsified regression guard: with identical history at the same tick, the reported records for a cardiac and a non-cardiac fault must differ **only** in `status` (the deliberate, already-published fault-kind channel) — verified to FAIL when the retired conditional is reintroduced, and the `count`/`slot` equalities are pinned to positive expected values so a do-nothing marshaller cannot satisfy it. Firmware host suite 28/28 green. **IEC 62304 Class C:** reporting-path change only — no stimulation enable, interlock threshold, cutoff timing or latch-commit behaviour altered; `np_fault_latch_commit()`/`_clear()`/`_init()` untouched. Development-mode posture: no SHDR fault records exist, so no fleet data was encoded under the retired rule and no consumer needed migration. See `docs/status/completed-decisions.md` (2026-08-12 entry).
- [x] **Fault latch extended SPI command privacy gate — RESOLVED (2026-07-14) — SUPERSEDED, see the entry immediately above:** The classification of the fault-latch `tick_ms` (event timestamp) and `count` fields — which are NOT in the current 8-byte `np_safety_tx_frame_t` reply and never leave SRAM today — is now locked for any future dedicated fault-latch SPI read command. **`count` → SHDR** unconditionally (distinct-fault-transition tally; device-condition, no biology, no timestamp; mirrors the SHDR device session count). **`tick_ms` → SHDR** in general (a device fault time, consistent with "safety interlock log → SHDR"), **but SUPPRESSED (reported as 0) when the latched fault carries `NP_SAFETY_STATUS_CARDIAC`** — a cardiac cutoff during cervical VNS co-locates the event time with a UHDR-class health event (the wearer's cardiac response), and even the relative SysTick value could be linked to a session record (mirrors the OI-CVNS-HUB-11 divergence flag's suppressed timestamp). Resolution baked into enforceable firmware, not prose: any future read command MUST marshal these fields through `np_fault_latch_report_tick_ms()` (CARDIAC→0) and `np_fault_latch_report_count()` in `firmware/safety_mcu/src/np_fault_latch.c` rather than reading `s_latch` directly. **18 host tests** (`firmware/safety_mcu/tests/np_fault_latch_privacy_tests.c`) assert the suppression + change-gated count, wired into `firmware/safety_mcu/CMakeLists.txt` + root `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` `add_test` + CI `firmware-host-tests.yml` (already triggers on `firmware/safety_mcu/**`); `np_fault_latch.c` cross-compiles clean for Cortex-M0+ under `-Werror` (section attribute guarded by `NPTEST_HOST` for host builds — Mach-O rejects the bare `.fault_latch` name; target image unchanged). Both elements added to the NP-FW-EMMC-001 §12 session data classification table (`docs/np_fw_emmc_001.docx`, now 32 elements; revision-history amendment logged). No change required to current firmware behavior — the 8-byte reply frame is untouched.

- [ ] **OI-BLE-01: SHDRUploader upgrade path to hub-provisioned TRNG warranty token** — **App-side IMPLEMENTED (iOS + Android, 2026-07-14); residual is external hub firmware OI-WA-03.** `SHDRUploader` now upgrades from its interim Keychain token to the hub-provisioned 256-bit TRNG token as soon as the hub delivers it over GATT. Wiring: the SHDR-facing `SHDRUploadTriggering` protocol gained `warrantyTokenPublisher: AnyPublisher<Data?, Never>` (backed by `NeurOneGATTManager.$warrantyToken`), keeping the uploader's least-privilege boundary (it still never sees the full UHDR-carrying GATT interface; the warranty token is SHDR-class device identity). `SHDRUploader.observeWarrantyToken()` subscribes and `compactMap`s out `nil` so a disconnect never rolls back an established upgrade; `upgradeDeviceToken(_:)` rejects any payload ≠ 32 bytes; `currentDeviceToken()` prefers the hub token (hex) for the `X-NP-Device-Token` header and falls back to the Keychain token before provisioning. The full app-side chain is closed (discovery → `readValue(for: warrantyTokenChar)` → `applyWarrantyToken` → `$warrantyToken` → uploader). +4 tests in `SHDRUploaderTests`. Until hub firmware ships the `warrantyToken` GATT characteristic (OI-WA-03, UUID `4E455550-0010-…`) the publisher stays `nil` and the Keychain fallback is used — the app has no work pending. BLOCKING for the full SHDR fleet DB no-join architecture (NP-FW-EMMC-002 Rev 1 §A) only insofar as OI-WA-03 must land. **Android — collector WIRED (2026-07-14, PR #204):** the Android SHDR upload pipeline is now composed to match iOS (superseding the earlier "not-yet-composed" status). `NeurOneGattManager` exposes `shdrUploadPending: StateFlow<Boolean>` (set true when `shdrUploadStatus` notifies 0x01, reset on disconnect), mirroring iOS `$shdrUploadPending` / `SHDRUploadTriggering`. `NeurOneApplication.composeShdrUploadPipeline()` instantiates `ShdrUploader` (gated by `WarrantyAnalyticsGate`) and launches two `bleScope` collectors: (a) `shdrUploadPending` → reads the hub-staged `shdr_staging.bin` payload on `Dispatchers.IO` and uploads when the warranty gate is open (deletes on success); (b) `warrantyToken` → `ShdrUploadWiring.applyWarrantyToken(it, …)` which drops nulls so a disconnect never downgrades the token. Token storage sits behind a `DeviceTokenStore` seam (`EncryptedPrefsDeviceTokenStore` in production — identical Keystore-encrypted CSPRNG behavior) so the token logic is JVM-testable without the Android Keystore; pure collector rules are in `ShdrUploadWiring`. Kotlin tests (`app/src/test/kotlin/life/neurone/app/data/ShdrUploaderTests.kt`) cover the four OI-BLE-01 cases (64-hex fallback, 32-byte upgrade, null-does-not-downgrade, non-32-byte rejected) plus two wiring-gate cases, build-verified by the new Android CI (`.github/workflows/android-ci.yml` → `:app:testDebugUnitTest`). Both platforms stay `nil` and use the CSPRNG/Keychain fallback until hub firmware ships `warrantyToken` (OI-WA-03) — the collectors then upgrade automatically. Tracked in app/ios/ISA.md Decisions as OI-BLE-01.

- [ ] **PBM concurrency: the tile ceiling is set by a heatsink no document specifies (NP-THERM-CFD-N1-001 Rev 1, 2026-09-03, against `OI-PWR-01`)** — Coupled 321-node N-tile network on the delivered 80-socket lattice, reproducing every published `NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001` temperature to ≤ 0.45 K from a single calibration point.
  - **(a) `OI-N1-02` — ~~BLOCKING~~ CLOSED 2026-09-08 by `NP-THERM-SINK-001`; see the new entry below.** All N tiles export ~90 % of their heat into **one** external heatsink at the via terminus. `NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001` §5 pins it at ambient as a modelling idealisation (*"perfect sink"*), `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.3 inherits that idealisation, and **no document in the tree gives it a thermal resistance** — `NP-HW-HUB-001` `OI-HUB-C19` treats it as a placement question with no number attached. It is the whole N-dependence: with the sink pinned the face moves **0.2 K from N = 1 to N = 80**; at 0.5 K/W it moves **17.6 K**. The concurrency ceiling ranges **16–78 tiles** at the library floor across a plausible heatsink range, and **0** at the R-4 point. **N1-D-1: no tile count may be quoted as a thermal ceiling until this closes.** Route with `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` F-04. **`R_sink` = 1.08 K/W, recovered twice and not chosen; the 16–78 range resolves to 10 tiles with a spreader and 2 without; `N1-D-1` is lifted and replaced by `SINK-D-1`. The successor BLOCKING item is `OI-SINK-01` — the exterior spreader.**
  - **(b) The "~6 tiles" rule and its thermal corroboration were both answering the wrong question.** `NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001`'s periodic cell has **adiabatic side walls**, which is the boundary condition of an *infinite array of identically-driven tiles*, not of one tile — the 80-tile model reproduces it to 6 × 10⁻¹⁴ K. So `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.2 spends a face margin that was never a function of tile count, and **the coincidence it records (a 4–8 thermal bracket straddling the power-derived ~6) does not survive a model with a heatsink in it.** The number 6 appears nowhere in the new ceiling table. **With an ideal sink there is no tile-count limit at all — only a per-tile drive wall** of 6.63 W/tile at 25 °C ambient, falling ~0.38 W/tile per °C to zero at ~42.5 °C. That is `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` **D-4**'s governor-in-watts arrived at independently from the thermal side.
  - **(c) 18 of 23 authored protocols are thermally bound, not power bound** — the reverse of `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §9's framing, computed against the same `analyse()` the power and dose checks use. **Four are inadmissible at one tile at any heatsink** (≥ 10 W/tile: Alzheimer's 1064 nm, Memory Boost, Vascular Baseline, Stroke rehab) — robust to every unspecified parameter, and they are the high-dose protocols, which connects to `OI-HEXTILE-21`'s emitter wall. **Fourteen become achievable on the heatsink alone**, which is the return on (a). **`OI-N1-03`: nothing stops a thermally inadmissible protocol compiling** — `hubCompiler.ts` has no thermal check, as it had no power check before `OI-HEXTILE-09`; the two should be one check in watts per tile.
  - **(d) `OI-PWR-10` recommended CLOSED — the answer is no.** Clustered against distributed, at equal N and equal power, is worth **≤ 0.05 K at the design point and ≤ 0.22 K at the envelope edge**, across every authored zone. `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.2's caveat had the mechanism backwards: the CFRP/µ-metal shell smears cavity residual heat laterally ~13× faster than it rejects it. What is wrong with the flat rule is its **unit**, not its lack of a geometry term.
  - **(e) One defect in the anchor document, raised not corrected (`OI-N1-01` / `OI-R1-06`).** R1 §5.1's published temperatures reproduce at a constant **0.649 ×** the flux its own table labels — consistent with η_wp applied twice to an `NP-THERM-CFD-001` §4 figure that is already `q_heat = P_elec(1 − η_wp)`. Non-conservative, and it lands on the exact number `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.2 divides: the T1-std nominal-ambient margin is **9.2 K, not 11.3 K**.
  - **(f) `OI-N1-04` — idle populated tiles conduct heat into the scalp** once ambient exceeds body temperature, through 80 vias from the shared sink. Scales with **population**, not activity, and no interlock in CLAUDE.md §4.2 observes an idle tile. Decide with `OI-HEXTILE-06`.
  - **(g) `OI-N1-08` — the ambient gate is a flat threshold against an N-dependent ceiling.** `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2 records the +35 °C PBM block as *"deliberately more conservative than the physics requires — `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §7 puts T1-std's full-dose ceiling at 37.9 °C."* **That 37.9 °C is a single-tile number.** §4b reproduces it at N = 1 (39.1–40.6 °C across the heatsink range — an independent corroboration), and finds the conservatism **inverts at N ≥ 20**: at a realistic 0.5 K/W the model admits only **32.9 °C at N = 20 and 12.2 °C at N = 80**, against a gate that passes anything up to 35 °C. Two honest bounds: it is conditional on `OI-N1-02` (with a perfect sink every row sits at ~39 °C and the gate is conservative at every N), and **it is not a live hazard**, because §6a's per-protocol ceilings bind first. It is a gate-design gap of the same shape as `OI-N1-03` — a safety gate whose input variable omits the term that moves the answer. Give the gate a concurrency or total-watts input, or bound N wherever the flat threshold is relied on; co-decide with `OI-OPR-01`'s derate curve, which `NP-THERM-COOL-001` D-4 has already given a per-protocol termination rule.
  **Folded-in items answered: `OI-PWR-08` (§3.5's sign and order confirmed; the figure is a property of drive and `R_sink`, not population), `OI-PWRSRC-07` (REFUTED — a 30-min session reaches 75–91 % of steady rise, not 39 %; its τ was R1's fan-off value), `OI-THCOOL-08` (`NP-THERM-COOL-001` §5's ratios shrink ~2.4×, RFE 3.28× → 1.36×, but §5's ordering and recommendation survive and the static gap bridge GFE is now the strongest row), `OI-PWRSRC-05` (stays flag-for-review, re-pointed at `OI-R1-02`).** `OI-PWR-01` **CLOSED 2026-09-15 by `NP-PWR-THERM-001` §4 (PWRTH-D-1)** — it was recommended NARROWED with the stated precondition *"it should not gate anything that `OI-N1-02` does not gate first"*, and that precondition was met when `OI-N1-02` closed on 2026-09-08. Nothing remains under its number that is not carried by name by `OI-R1-01`, `OI-R1-02` or `OI-SINK-01`. Seven decisions `N1-D-1…7` (two to principal), four risk rows, seven open items `OI-N1-01…07` (one BLOCKING). **No locked section modified; no firmware, app or protocol changed.** See `docs/np_therm_cfd_n1_001.md`.
- [ ] **The heatsink `OI-N1-02` asks for does not exist, and specifying what is actually there costs most of the tile ceiling (NP-THERM-SINK-001 Rev 1, 2026-09-08, closes `OI-N1-02`)** — Refined resistance network on the same 80-socket lattice, adding one node: the **exterior skin**. Reproduces `NP-THERM-CFD-R1-001`'s own cell to 1.8 × 10⁻¹⁴ K in compatibility mode, and re-partitions `R_OUT_BASE` exactly (0.230 + 0.065 + 0.115 = 0.410) — no resistance is invented.
  - **(a) There is no heatsink to specify, and no fan on this path.** R1's via is a **~32 mm radial conductor** terminating on the **outer bowl**; the hub `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` §2 infers is a median **188 mm** away and nothing connects them. A solid collection trunk drops **40 K at the N = 6 library floor per 100 g of copper** (193 K at the R-4 point) against a **17.0 K total face budget**; a heatsink bolted on at the occiput adds **zero tiles even at `R_hub` = 0**. **`SINK-D-2`: none is to be specified.** The useful corollary: **`OI-HUB-C19` is decoupled** from the tile field and may close on hub electronics alone (`SINK-D-3`).
  - **(b) `SPEC-SINK-01` — `R_sink` = 1.08 K/W aggregate** (band 0.83–1.58), recovered **twice**: from a decomposition of R1's own `R_cav→amb` (h 8.18 W/m²K over the real exterior) and independently from a Churchill sphere correlation plus linearised radiation over the 0.118 m² vault exterior (7.81). **Two derivations sharing no input, 4.7 % apart.** N1's sweep bracketed the truth; its **0.50 "plausible default" was 2.2× optimistic** and its 1.00 row is the real one.
  - **(c) R1 spends the same external film twice.** Reproducing its cell requires **both** pinning the skin at ambient *and* charging the cavity leg the full 0.18 with the film still in it. This is the **third** accounting defect found on the same face margin, after `OI-N1-01` (η_wp twice, 11.3 K → 9.2 K) and N1 §3 (the margin was never a function of tile count). None of the three makes the design better.
  - **(d) The missing component is a SPREADER, not a heatsink (`SINK-D-4`, `SPEC-SINK-04`).** A lumped `R_sink` presumes an isothermal terminus; the bare CFRP bowl spreads over ~63 mm and is eighty hot spots. **Ceiling at the library floor and 25 °C: 10 tiles with a 100 µm pyrolytic-graphite film (28 g), 2 without** — against N1's 16–78. In watts, **8.9 W at N = 6 with the spreader against 4.7 W without**, converging on 31.4 W fully distributed. **`OI-SINK-01` is the new BLOCKING item**: the film needs a BOM line against a margin-negative T1, a lamination process, and an EMF/ingress clearance this study is not entitled to assert. **EMF half NARROWED 2026-09-23, not cleared (`NP-THERM-SINK-001` Rev 2 §7.1, GitHub #399, closing `OI-EMCCAV-02`):** Helmholtz eddy loading cleared at first order at the 20 dB mid share across the ELF band and at the 30 dB top share up to ~230 Hz (ε ≤ 0.041 at 300 Hz; `NP-THERM-BOWL-001` §5.2's budget); external RF open at the film's terminations only (`EMF-1-SINK-2`); the film needs a cut-out at the §4.3 TMS window (`OI-SINK-09`). **Still BLOCKING.**
  - **(e) The R-4 point is inadmissible, robustly.** Across a 6–14 W/m²K × 0.090–0.150 m² box the ceiling at 6.25 W/tile is **0 in every cell** — it needs none of the soft inputs, because the 4.29 K/W via alone exceeds the face budget at that drive. The **library-floor** ceiling is *not* robust (4 → 25 across the same box) and is a THERM-1b target, not a claim.
  - **(f) A term no prior document carries: the exterior sits 6.1 K above ambient with every tile idle.** Eighty populated sockets conduct **5.6 W** from the 37 °C perfused core into the shell at 25 °C ambient. Unremarkable as physiology, consequential as budget. `OI-N1-04` should be restated to cover both signs (`OI-SINK-06`).
  - **(g) `SPEC-SINK-03`: fan loss does not change `R_sink`, and `RISK-26`'s named cause is wrong.** The fault case is **vault occlusion** — a hood, bedding, a pillow under a supine user — which crosses 42 °C at φ ≥ 0.9 at N = 6 on the library floor and which **fan RPM cannot observe**, so `SR-FAN-05`'s predictive claim does not reach it. `NP-REQ-FANHEALTH-001` Path B1's face NTC still covers the hazard: the **control** holds, the **cause list** does not. `OI-SINK-04`.
  **Left deliberately untouched, and both since discharged 2026-09-15 by `NP-PWR-THERM-001`:** no figure in `NP-THERM-CFD-N1-001` is rewritten and `check-thermal-multitile.ts`'s `R_SINK_DEFAULT` stays at 0.5 (`OI-SINK-02` — **CLOSED by pairing rather than overwrite**: `R_SINK_SPECIFIED = 1.08` added alongside, with `rSinkNote()` printed at every report line naming either, because the defect was a superseded number being readable *without its label*, not a constant having the wrong value); N1 §6a's per-protocol table needs re-running first, because "recoverable on the heatsink alone" is no longer an available move (`OI-SINK-05` — **CLOSED**: **22 of 22** zone-fixed protocols are thermally bound, **3** inadmissible at any rejection resistance, **4** recovered by the *spreader*, and **21 of 22 hold no tiles at all on the bare shell that ships**). Eight open items `OI-SINK-01…08` (one BLOCKING), five risk rows, six decisions `SINK-D-1…6` (three to principal). Adds `scripts/check-thermal-sink.ts`.

- [ ] **Power-source architecture: the wall plug is premature for T1, and the T1/T2 power gate rests on a premise that has since been invalidated (NP-PWRSRC-001 Rev 1, 2026-08-27)** — Costed design study against the principal direction of 2026-08-27 (multi-source supply, purchase-time selection, thermal injury as a time-temperature dose, a T1/T2 protocol split, and whether the power supply can itself be the tier gate). Adds `scripts/check-power-source-coverage.ts` and `scripts/check-thermal-dose.ts`, both importing `analyse()` from `check-pbm-power.ts` so demand, coverage and dose cannot fork. **Recommended T1 BOM delta $0.00.** Four things for the principal:
  - **(a) `OI-PWRSRC-22` — principal. The T1/T2 power gate is a sound mechanism whose premise moved.** It was decided against a coverage ladder in which more watts unlocked 16, then 21, then 22 of 23 protocols. **`NP-PWRSRC-001` §4 restates `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §4.4.4's argument in watts instead of tiles and it stops being a limit and becomes a wall: the sealed cavity supports 27.6–49.1 W of aggregate emitter power and the existing 45 W brick already delivers 40 W of it.** PD 240 W EPR would deliver 4.7–8.4× what the assembly can reject; 1,800 W mains, 36–65×. **After the ceiling, every rung of the ladder unlocks the same two protocols — thermally achievable coverage is 2/23 under every candidate source, identical to today.** Net protocols unlocked by a T2 mains accessory: **zero**, and **zero of the thirteen risky ones**, which are already reachable on a stock Home Standard by cascading. **So the gate would buy an IEC 60601-1 isolation barrier at the 510(k) tier (with `VE-11` Open) plus a $210–380 accessory whose failure ends a clinic session, for no protocols.** Recommendation **D-15: do not buy the barrier for this.** What the direction wants — supervised-only protocols — is delivered in full by a **device-bound signed entitlement at $0.00 BOM** (§6.7), which can also express the duration and dose axes a supply cannot see.
  - **(b) `OI-PWRSRC-01` — cascading converts the power problem into a thermal-dose problem, and nothing checks it.** Every thermal control in the design is a real-time **reactive** interlock; a repo-wide search finds **no CEM43 or cumulative thermal-dose treatment on `main`**. The audit returns two clean nulls and one finding: protocols that fit the envelope reach **3.5 × 10⁻⁶ CEM43 over a full 108-session course**, and back-to-back sessions peak at 37.9 °C — *both negligible* — while **cascading, `NP-SES-PWR-001` §4's own sanctioned remedy for insufficient power, holds the cavity at the interlock ceiling for hours: Vascular Baseline 40 groups, 20.0 h, 292 CEM43, with 13 of 20 cascaded protocols past the conservative reference line.** The 42 °C interlock caps **temperature** and has no **duration** input, so it cannot bound dose. Ambient 25 → 35 °C moves a single session's dose ~500,000×, making `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001`'s gate the most load-bearing thermal control in the design. **All thermal constants are provisional named exports — flag-for-review, not a pass/fail gate, until `OI-PWR-01` lands (`OI-PWRSRC-05`).**
  - **(c) Three BLOCKING items that pre-exist this study.** `OI-PWRSRC-10` — *"the certified charger is the barrier"* does not identify **which standard** certified it: a commodity PD brick is IEC 62368-1 (ITE) and the device has conductive applied parts under a binding IEC 60601-1, and **this applies to the inlet that ships today**, BLOCKING for `VE-11`. `OI-PWRSRC-11` — **the hub's hold-up is undimensioned**: no battery, the 22 F supercapacitor is specified for duty-cycle transients, and CLAUDE.md §3's hardware-enforced 30 s tDCS ramp is certainly longer than any plausible hold-up, BLOCKING for `OI-PWR-13`. `OI-PWRSRC-12` — **the thermal budget is not a number the governor can read**, so a watts-only governor would permit a 232 W protocol the assembly cannot reject; use the conservative 28 W until it exists.
  - **(d) Premise corrections of record.** The commissioning demand list carried a phantom 40 W entry and duplicated a 200 W row. **Superseded in part, 2026-08-28 — `OI-PWRSRC-24`:** the study also reported the maximum as **1,600 W**, explaining 1,775 W away as an `OI-PBMCH-04` compiler artefact on an unmerged branch. **Both limbs are wrong at HEAD.** (i) `clinical-03-pbm-alzheimers-1064.npps` merged as **PR #296** (`f8806dd`) during the study's run, so **1,775 W is on `main`**; the claim was true of the base commit `d817364` and was falsified by a merge. (ii) The number is **not** a compiler artefact — `OI-PBMCH-04` concerns `wavelength` failing to reach the wire on **iOS and Windows**, whereas 25.0 W/tile comes from the **web** path, which reported that same 25.0 W/tile for the other 1064 nm protocol on `main` *before* #296 (8 × 25.0 = 200 W). 25.0 W/tile is simply the 1064 nm per-tile rate; the 200 W → 1,775 W difference is **zone size** (8 vs 71 sockets), a deliberate authoring decision the principal declined to narrow. **The lesson is the inverse of the programme's standing rule:** *"does not exist"* is uncheckable and *"not on `main`"* is checkable — but a claim about `main` **has a shelf life while work is in flight**, so it must name its commit and be re-checked before the document lands. **§4's conclusion is untouched:** 1,600 W and 1,775 W are both 33–64× the 27.6–49.1 W cavity ceiling.
  **Sixteen decisions `D-1…D-16` (five to principal), twelve risk rows of which four have no verification defined, twenty-four open items `OI-PWRSRC-01…24`. No locked section modified; no firmware, app or protocol changed.** See `docs/np_pwrsrc_001.md`.
- [ ] **A second power inlet alongside USB-C is a third TMS power-path option nobody assessed — and CLAUDE.md §2.2 may already assume it (NP-PWR-BUDGET-001 Rev 3, 2026-08-21)** — `OI-PWR-04` framed the TMS power path as shared-rail vs. mains-tethered. A third option exists: **keep USB-C for data, programming and all T1 power; add a second power-only inlet for high-draw T2 sessions.** It dominates both because it **scopes the Mode 3 loss to the modality that forces it** rather than to the whole device — CLAUDE.md §1's *"wired-first USB-C"* and Mode 3 autonomy both survive for T1. Four decisions:
  - **(a) `OI-PWR-11` — principal, and it comes first. §2.2 (LOCKED) ships Pro Full two 65 W chargers ($26 BOM); §4.5 negotiates a single 100 W EPR contract. 130 W is not 100 W.** §2.2's table is otherwise strictly keyed to *"peak draw of configuration"* and prices spares nowhere else. Either the second brick is an undocumented spare, or **a dual-inlet architecture was assumed and never specified**, or it powers a **separate T2 accessory that has no port, enclosure or power spec in any document** — the last being consistent with the finding that TMS is absent from the electrical model entirely. **The answer decides whether §4.4 is a proposal or a specification.** Nothing in §2.2 has been modified.
  - **(b) Prefer a second USB-C PD sink over a barrel or proprietary DC inlet — and the reason is IEC 60601-1, not cost.** A PD sink stays SELV behind a certified charger; a mains-derived inlet makes **NeurOne the isolation barrier** on a device with conductive applied parts (tDCS, tACS, VNS), at the 510(k) tier, with `NP-DT-001` **VE-11** (accredited-lab standards testing, G3 Month 10–14) still **Open**. It also reuses the already-specified USB-C Layer 5 filter and preserves §2.2's *"any PD-compliant charger must work"* EU stance. `NP-TOOL-HUB-001` HUB-MDR-04 already specifies two hub port openings with tethered covers, so the mechanical feature exists.
  - **(c) `OI-PWR-12` — EMF must be bench-verified, not argued.** An inlet carrying up to 5 A of switched current is a materially worse aggressor than a data port, and the hub PCB sits at or adjacent to the **occipital arch**, inside the assembly whose **measured** shielding is the product's primary technical claim. **Gates the option regardless of how (a) resolves.**
  - **(d) `OI-PWR-13` — dual-source arbitration is new IEC 62304 surface.** OR-ing, inrush, and defined behaviour on mid-session hot-plug or removal; a brownout during active stimulation is a safety event. **The safety MCU has no representation of total available power today.** Interacts with D-4 — the governor's budget becomes the sum of two negotiated contracts, recomputed on hot-plug.
  **It does NOT raise T1 PBM concurrency**, and should not be justified on that basis: §3.2's thermal ceiling (4–8 tiles) and the power-derived one (~6) are coincident, so relieving power just moves the binding constraint to heat. Revisit only if `OI-PWR-01`'s multi-tile CFD returns a materially higher thermal ceiling. See `docs/np_pwr_budget_001.md` §4.4.
  **RESOLVED 2026-09-15 (`NP-PWR-THERM-001` §11–§12, PWRTH-D-7/D-8) — the third option is assessed and declined for a fourth, and `OI-PWR-11` is answered.** With `SPEC-TMS-04`'s **176 W** the option set has a number for the first time. **(c)'s whole case was scoping the Mode 3 loss, and no power bank sources 176 W**, so TMS is outside Mode 3 by arithmetic whatever the inlet count is — the case evaporates while `OI-PWR-12`'s cost (a 5 A switched aggressor inside the measured-shielding assembly) stands. **Resolution: the PD inlet goes on the TMS driver's own enclosure**, where §8.4's 33 W of winding dissipation and `SPEC-TMS-02`'s 1,600 V bank already are; the head-worn hub stays single-inlet, so `OI-PWR-12` does not arise, the path stays SELV, and `OI-PWR-13` is unneeded for the head-worn assembly. A shared single 240 W EPR rail is the fallback and is **feasible now** — §4.3 rejected it against an *unbounded* requirement, not a large one. **`OI-PWR-11`: 2 × 65 = 130 W against 176 W, short by 36 %, so reading 2 (a dual-inlet architecture assumed) is REFUTED** and §4.4 was a proposal, not a specification being rediscovered; the residual choice between "spare" and "1170 nm applicator supply" is commercial (`OI-PWRTH-03`). **§2.2 remains locked and unmodified.** The open successor is `OI-PWRTH-07` — passive or active applicator — which decides (d) vs the fallback.

- [ ] **Hub NVRAM HAL — the store Map 3 needs, and the filesystem under every atomicity claim — PARTIALLY RESOLVED 2026-09-13 (NP-FW-NVRAM-001 Rev 2 + NP-SOUP-LFS-001 Rev 1, Issue #339); item stays open on (c) and (d)** — Design study specifying `OI-HEXMAP-01` against the four-map principal decision of 2026-08-25. Twenty decisions, ten risk rows. **No new part and $0.00 BOM delta at 20 or 30 tiles.** Fifteen open items, `OI-NVRAM-01…15`; three are blocking. See `docs/np_fw_nvram_001.md`. **Rev 2 (2026-09-13) makes the store buildable and hands the SOUP half to its own record.** `OI-NVRAM-01` **decided and downgraded from BLOCKING to a documentation action** — the Config journal is LittleFS files, and `EMMC-CFG-02`'s raw-write region is void with its *justification refuted rather than outweighed*: at `EMMC-FS-01`'s `prog_size` of 256 B an append costs one 256-byte program inside the current block, and **a raw region cannot make the program unit smaller, because the unit belongs to the eMMC and not to the filesystem.** What the clause was aimed at is `np_module_map_persist()` rewriting 14,012 bytes to record a 16-byte fact — the 4.6-year endurance figure — and **D-5 already removes that** by giving Map 3 its own file. The clause was aimed at the right symptom and the wrong cause. Two further grounds against the alternative: shrinking `block_count` **relocates the UKMD record**, whose loss makes a user's UHDR permanently unmountable with no NeurOne-held second copy (free today, but the window closes at first *provisioning*, not first ship — the same boundary error §7.2 corrects for the version bump); and a raw region means re-implementing commit ordering, per-record CRC and wear levelling by hand at Class B, which is the wrong answer to `OI-NVRAM-12`. **Also closed:** `OI-NVRAM-02` (D-22 — the UKMD record becomes a named LittleFS file; `NP-FW-EMMC-002` → Rev 3 §C.3, provably safe because `np_uhdr_key.c` has always reached it through `np_uhdr_hal_config_read_ukmd()` and never through the `0x1000` offset), `OI-NVRAM-11` (`np_module_map.h`'s sizing comment, which carried both the v2 figures **and** the retired `0x1000` address), and `OI-NVRAM-13` (D-24 — 32-byte record so eight fit one program unit and none straddles it, 49,152-byte file, 1,536 rows, ~19 per socket, against a stated one-row-per-module-per-session assumption whose clinic-cadence margin of **1.6 days is stated rather than rounded away** and is bounded by §6.3's degrade-to-totals-plus-`detail_lost`, not by a failure). `OI-NVRAM-03` **decided** — widen the Config specification, do not forbid the write, as Rev 1 predicted. `NP-FW-EMMC-001` is a `.docx` (`OI-CONV-04`) and is **not** edited; its three clause changes are raised as **`ECR-EMMC-001`** with replacement text (§3.3.1.4). **What keeps this item open: (c) `OI-NVRAM-05`, untouched, still blocking `NP-MOD-ID-001` §10's factory-reset rotation test — a factory-reset correctness question, not a storage-layer one; and (d) `OI-NVRAM-10`, carried forward and now with a named consequence** (see (b)).
  - **(a) `OI-NVRAM-01` — ~~BLOCKING~~ → DECIDED 2026-09-13 (Rev 2 §3.3.1, D-21); now a documentation action only. `EMMC-CFG-02`'s raw-write journal area has no address space.** `NP-FW-EMMC-001` reserves *"a dedicated Config partition journal area (raw-write, no LittleFS overhead for high-frequency updates)"* for the session counter, lockout state and PD profile — and `EMMC-FS-01` gives Config's LittleFS instance `block_size` 4,096 × `block_count` 4,096 = **16 MiB, the whole partition**. Raw writes into those blocks are filesystem corruption. Either shrink `block_count` to carve the journal out, or make the journal LittleFS files and void the clause's own rationale. Map 3's append substrate depends on the answer, and so does `OI-NVRAM-02` (`NP-FW-EMMC-002` §C.3's *"Config partition offset 0x1000 — UKMD record"*, an offset into the same managed blocks). **Resolved as above; Map 3 is implementable against NP-FW-NVRAM-001 Rev 2 now.** Original wording retained for the record.
  - **(b) `OI-NVRAM-12` — SUPERSEDED 2026-09-13 by `NP-SOUP-LFS-001` Rev 1; the blocking half continues as `OI-LFS-01`. LittleFS is the only Class B SOUP item that is neither vendored, version-pinned, nor anomaly-evaluated.** `NP-SW-001` §9.4 records it as *"2.x"* with one line of verification, against per-file SHA-256 provenance records and IEC 62304 §7.1.2 evaluations for FreeRTOS, Monocypher, CMSIS-Core and CMSIS-Device; nothing exists under `firmware/vendor/`. **Every power-loss guarantee in the storage architecture is asserted against a component with no configuration identity.** **The component is not merely unmanaged — it is absent.** `grep -rn "lfs_" firmware/` returns **two hits, both comments** in `np_log_backend.h` naming `lfs_file_write`/`lfs_file_sync` as what the `OI-LOG-06/07` HAL seams will call. No source under `firmware/vendor/`, nothing links it, nothing calls it: **nominated** SOUP, not integrated SOUP. `NP-SW-001` → Rev 5; its cell had read *"2.x"* / *"Power-loss testing per LittleFS test suite"*, which names no version and records that an upstream project tests its own code — **that cell is the defect**, because a SOUP verification entry must record what NeurOne did. **The IEC 62304 §7.1.2 evaluation is deliberately NOT recorded**: the clause evaluates the anomaly list for the version *actually in use*, and none is; a conclusion against a major-version family would be a record that resolves to nothing — the `OI-DOC-01` failure reproduced inside the document written to close its sibling. The blocking action is recorded instead. **The hazard analysis IS performed** and is version-independent, because what a filesystem anomaly can *reach* is a property of the callers. Three consumer families: the session log (nothing on the device reads it back — records loss, not emission); UID-keyed calibration (**refuses to fall back**, so corruption becomes no-drive rather than wrong-drive); and the `"NPMP"` blob, which carries Map 1's per-element **safe ranges** and is the one path toward drive magnitude. **The finding that outlives integration: Class B holds, but on a property of `np_module_map`'s reject-and-rebuild policy and not of LittleFS** — a CRC failure discards the blob and re-enumerates from the sockets, so every integrity failure leaves the inventory *empty*, never *wrong*. `NP-FW-NVRAM-001` §7.2 specifies **Map 3 to need the opposite policy** (tail-additive forever) for independently correct reasons. Both decisions are right; together they make the classification load-bearing on which file is which. Hence **`REQ-LFS-01`: no value that bounds an emission may be stored under a tail-additive policy** — breaching it makes `OI-NVRAM-10`'s thermal-cutoff-standing-in-for-an-optical-limit the *only* remaining bound rather than the third of three. Continues as **`OI-LFS-01`** (pin + vendor — **still BLOCKING** any reliance on `NP-FW-NVRAM-001` §4 or `NP-FW-HUB-001` §6.5, and blocking #75/`NP-SBOM-001`, since an SBOM entry reading `2.x` is not an SBOM entry), **`OI-LFS-02`** (the §7.1.2 evaluation plus a NeurOne power-loss injection test against claims `L-1…L-4`, falsified in both directions first per `NP-CONV-001` §8) and **`OI-LFS-03`** (the CI check that makes `REQ-LFS-01` observable rather than remembered).

    > **UPDATE 2026-09-14 — `OI-LFS-01` is CLOSED, and the blocking status MOVED rather than lifted (`NP-SOUP-LFS-001` Rev 2, `NP-SW-001` Rev 6, DHF Rev 50).** littlefs is pinned at **`v2.11.3`** (tag commit `6cb4e865`) and vendored at `firmware/vendor/littlefs/`: five byte-exact files with per-file SHA-256 verified by two independent downloads, a `VERSION` SOUP record on the `cmsis_core` pattern, and §7.3's configuration recorded, compiled and tested as first-party code in `hub_control` (`LFS_NO_MALLOC` + static buffers, `LFS_THREADSAFE` on, assertions retargeted to a halt that hands control to the safety MCU, `block_cycles` **500** checked *by value* because `-1` passes every non-zero test and silently disables `L-6`, and `np_lfs_config_validate()` as the mount-time check `L-5` names). **The tag is chosen on a safety argument rather than recency**: `v2.11.3` is the first release carrying upstream `488e84bb`, which fixes data corruption from two open write handles on one file — exactly the axis `L-1…L-4` rest on. New host target `np_lfs_config_tests` (Class B 27 → 28, repo 34 → 35), 19 falsified rejections, falsified in seven ways per `NP-CONV-001` §8.
>
    > **Read the next sentence before citing any of that.** Nothing calls the library (`OI-LOG-05..07` are unimplemented), IEC 62304 §7.1.2 is **still undischarged**, and no power-loss behaviour has been exercised. `NP-FW-NVRAM-001` §4, `EMMC-FS-01` and `NP-FW-HUB-001` §6.5 are exactly as unverified as before; **`OI-LFS-02` inherited the BLOCKING status**, and both documents were amended so neither reads as unblocked. A populated vendor directory is not evidence — `NP-SOUP-LFS-001` §7.5. **#75 (`NP-SBOM-001`) IS unblocked.** Rev 1's reason for not pinning is **outweighed, not refuted**: "do not pin before the anomaly list is read" blocked its own precondition, since the list cannot be read without a tag. Two new open items — **`OI-LFS-05`** (`EMMC-FS-01` states parameters for the **Config** partition only, and `L-1`/`L-2` are claims about the **log** partitions, whose `block_count` is 131,072 and 1,767,168 against Config's 4,096) and **`OI-LFS-06`** (make "one open handle per file" checkable rather than remembered). `RISK-LFS-04` closed, `RISK-LFS-05` added. **`REQ-LFS-01` and the §6.2 conclusion are untouched** — the Class B classification is still a property of `np_module_map`'s reject-and-rebuild policy, and pinning a version could not change that.
>
    > **UPDATE 2026-09-14 (second) — `OI-LFS-02` is CLOSED, and what remains is narrower and is named (`NP-SOUP-LFS-001` Rev 3 §11 and §12, `NP-SW-001` Rev 7, DHF Rev 51).** The IEC 62304 §7.1.2 anomaly evaluation against `v2.11.3` is **performed**: thirteen items from the upstream tracker and release notes assessed against claims `L-1…L-7`, **three applicable, none on a path to an emission** — and only one of the three (#1086, which becomes the retargeted halt the safety MCU converts into a stimulation cutoff) is stopped by something NeurOne has already built. #1205 is stopped by the callers' CRC/AEAD checks; **#1210 is stopped by nothing** — compaction can drop a file's `NAME` tag while keeping its data and a valid CRC, and the worst case is `ukmd.rec`, whose loss makes a user's UHDR permanently unmountable. The power-loss injection test is **written, run and falsified in both directions**: `np_lfs_powerloss_tests` (Class B 28 → 29, repo 35 → 36), NeurOne's own injector, a cut swept across every medium-touching op of three commit sequences under three tear models — **456 interrupted runs, 0 violations** — with three unsafe orderings required to be caught and six hand perturbations of which five were caught and one (clearing the `lfs_t` across a simulated reboot) was not, and is recorded as a negative result. **`NP-FW-NVRAM-001` §4, `EMMC-FS-01` and `NP-FW-HUB-001` §6.5 are unblocked exactly as far as `NP-SOUP-LFS-001` §12.5 states**: against the `struct lfs_config` **contract**, not against the eMMC behind the XTS layer. **Three successors, all open:** `OI-LFS-07` (power-loss injection on hardware — the part of `OI-LFS-02` a host could never discharge; upstream #1083 asks the same question and has no answer), `OI-LFS-08` (#1210: bound the Config directory's create/delete churn and stop `ukmd.rec` resting on one filesystem entry) and `OI-LFS-09` (validate stored values by content on every read — `lfs_stat()` is not a durability check). `OI-LFS-05` and `OI-LFS-06` are unchanged and still open; nothing calls the library.
  - **(c) `OI-NVRAM-05` — BLOCKING for `NP-MOD-ID-001` §10. Two SNVS flags are documented as power-loss recovery and can only be warm-reset recovery.** The SNVS low-power domain has no `VBAT` supply on a device with no battery or coin cell, so `NP_SNVS_RESET_IN_PROGRESS` is cleared by exactly the event it exists to detect: **a power loss during factory-reset steps R-4…R-9 goes undetected and the device boots with UHDR/SHDR/Config half-erased**, which `np_main.c`'s own comment says must never happen. `NP_SNVS_ANON_IN_PROGRESS` has the same defect with a benign residual (Scratch is zeroed every boot regardless).
  - **(d) `OI-NVRAM-10` — is there a Class C bound on per-tile emitter drive current independent of Map 1's ranges?** The SW-02 Class B classification rests on no stored value being able to widen a Class C permission. That holds for stimulation *enable*. If a tile variant's maximum emitter current is bounded only by Map 1's `[min : incr : max]`, a wrong or substituted range is bounded only by the 62 °C thermal cutoff — a thermal limit standing in for an optical one — and the classification must be re-derived before any such variant ships. **Sharpened 2026-09-13:** `NP-SOUP-LFS-001` §5.3 makes this the *third of three* barriers between a stored-value defect and an emission — blob CRC, then reject-and-rebuild, then the 62 °C throttle — and `REQ-LFS-01` is what keeps the second one in place. `NP-FW-HUB-001` §9 carries the same residual from the hub side. If either is ever breached this is the only bound left, and it is a thermal limit standing in for an optical one.
  - **(e) `OI-NVRAM-09` — a per-window delta is a timestamped count, and `TIME-01` cannot see it.** Map 3's natural output is *"everything since the last sync"*, a delta whose interval ends only the control software can date — and it holds a clock. Uploading `(uid, Δ, window_end_ordinal)` reconstructs per-socket usage over calendar time without any time-typed column, i.e. it walks around `TIME-01` rather than breaching it, and would remove the absence `NP-MOD-ID-001` §7.5.1.1 makes *"the foundation of this consent model"*. Rule adopted: **totals only to SHDR, never boundaries or deltas; Map 2 is app-side and no code path may read it and write SHDR.** Needs a CI check on the `warranty-nojoin-ci.yml` pattern.
  **Four premise corrections of record.** Map 4's substrate is **not** open and **not** inside `OI-HEXTILE-06` (a photodiode-population decision) — `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` D-3 and `NP-MOD-ID-001` MODID-4 already specify it inside a tinyAVR already costed at $0.50/tile; the module-change power cut is a per-cluster `SAFE_EN[n]` **emitter-rail** cut, not a hub power loss; **`SEAT#` cannot supply a last-gasp write window** (no firmware consumer, Class B aggregation, undimensioned pad stagger — `OI-NVRAM-04`); and the free-version-bump window closes at the **first unsynced Map 3 row**, not at first ship, because reject-and-rebuild is right for a cache and destroys a record. **Cost of not doing it:** without persistence, UID-keyed dose calibration falls back to `NP_CAL_DEFAULT` every boot, rendering inert the **$11.53/tile driver-plus-metering line — $346/headset at 30 tiles** — which CLAUDE.md §3 sells as the primary differentiator over Vielight. Remaining items: `OI-NVRAM-03` (the `"NPMP"` blob is written during normal operation and appears in neither `EMMC-CFG-01`'s contents nor `-02`'s whitelist), `-06` (a session interrupted by tile extraction contributes nothing to that tile's odometer), `-07` (ordinal-indexed trajectories inherit `OI-EMMC2-13`), `-08` (CI check that a journal survives a version bump), `-11` (`np_module_map.h`'s sizing comment still carries the v2 figure 17,804 against the code's 22,412), `-13` (Map 3's size bound is unchosen), `-14` (CLAUDE.md §3 still names the retired ATtiny402 where `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §6.2 selects ATtiny426/427-class, and Map 4 is sized at exactly 128 B against the latter), `-15` (`NP-MOD-ID-001` is DRAFT and this study builds on MODID-4/5/6).

- [ ] **17 of 20 predefined PBM protocols exceed the power envelope, and all 20 compile clean (NP-SES-PWR-001 Rev 1 + NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 Rev 8, 2026-08-21)** — `OI-HEXTILE-09` was raised as a hypothetical gap in the v2 wire format. It is an actual one: audited against `protocols/predefined/`, **2 protocols fit the 40 W emitter budget, 17 exceed it by 1.25× to 40×** (Vascular Baseline needs 1,600 W), **1 is operator-scoped**. `app/web/src/lib/hubCompiler.ts` has **no power or budget check of any kind**. Audit: `bun scripts/check-pbm-power.ts`. Four decisions follow:
  - **(a) `OI-SESPWR-01` — the zones are lobe-scale; the evidence is electrode-scale. This is a clinical-validity defect, not a power one.** `clinical-04-pbm-depression.npps` is titled *"PBM — Depression (DLPFC)"* and targets **37 sockets**; its cited evidence specifies *bilateral DLPFC (F3/F4)*. **No DLPFC zone, no F3/F4 zone, and no single-10-20-site zone exists** — the finest frontal targeting available is a 20-socket half-lobe. One tile is ~40 mm FWHM at cortex (`NP-OPT-PSF-001` §3.2), so ~2 sockets/hemisphere is both physically meaningful and evidence-faithful. **This would need fixing even with an unlimited power budget** — a protocol titled DLPFC that irradiates the frontal lobe cannot support a DLPFC claim, which is exactly the hazard `clinical-09-pbm-stroke-rehab.npps` already names in its own comments and resolves with `clinician_selected`. **Blocked on REG-1** (socket-to-10-20 registration). **Largest single reduction in the over-budget condition, and it is a data edit.**
  - **(b) `OI-SESPWR-03` — `frequency: 0Hz` with `duty_cycle:` is undefined and worth 4×.** Four protocols set both. `NP-NPPS-REF-001` §4.1 says `frequency: 0` selects CW and CW is 100 % duty; `hubCompiler.ts` emits `freqCode(0)` and `dutyReg(25)` independently and leaves the hub to decide. Depression draws **740 W or 185 W** depending on the answer. **This blocks `OI-HEXTILE-09`** — a governor cannot be written against an undefined input. Preferred resolution: make it a parser error.
  - **(c) `OI-SESPWR-02` — possible R-4 CW breach, independent of power.** `Vascular Baseline` at 80 % CW implies ~322 mW/cm² against R-4's 200 mW/cm² CW ceiling. No mode-dependent clamp was found in the compiler, the validator, or on the firmware searches run — **which is not proof of absence.** Needs a firmware read.
  - **(d) `OI-SESPWR-04` — cascading cannot rescue the protocols that most need it.** Rotating socket groups preserves dose and multiplies session length by the group count (40 m to **20 h**). It survives for dose-driven CW protocols — and **fast cascading is the same mechanism as `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.6's whole-vault mode**, in the time domain rather than the current domain. It **invalidates every 40 Hz entrainment protocol**, including Grade A Alzheimer's, because the claimed mechanism is network-wide *coherent* rhythmic drive; it also breaks closed-loop adaptation and the cross-modal 40 Hz phase-lock in `15-full-t1-immersive`. **Any cascade primitive must carry a per-protocol admissibility flag defaulting to *not admissible*** — without it, the primitive silently invalidates the strongest protocols in the library.
  **Sequence matters (`NP-SES-PWR-001` §5): fix the zones, then the CW/duty semantic, then the CW ceiling, THEN build the governor, and cascade only the residue.** A governor built against today's library would reject 17 protocols when the correct response to 15 of them is to fix the protocol. See `docs/np_ses_pwr_001.md`.

- [ ] **PBM has an efficacy FLOOR, not just a ceiling — and three things follow (NP-PWR-BUDGET-001 Rev 2 + NP-HW-HEXTILE-001 Rev 7, 2026-08-21)** — The power-budget study classified every modality by its *ceiling* (dose-capped or heat-removal-bound). **A budget bounded only from above cannot say what the power is for.** `docs/pbm_neuro_protocols.md` supplies the floor: **0.02–0.3 W/cm² at scalp, 10–120 J/cm², 6–30 min**, with *"under-dosing, not mechanism failure, explains most nulls"* (NEST-1/2/3; Iosifescu 2022). Three consequences now need decisions:
  - **(a) `OI-HEXTILE-06` has arguments on both sides, and the cost model sees only one.** *Populated* is not *driven* — a populated socket costs 2 mA / 25 mW standby plus BOM, not 25 W, so a fully populated helmet under the ~6-tile governor draws what a 20-tile build draws. Options 2/3 (silicon PD, per-cluster PD) **trade away the dual-PD dose metering**, which is the structural answer to the **−79 % declared-vs-measured** gap independently measured on a marketed 1070 nm helmet — dropping it to save ~$9/tile makes NeurOne a better-specified competitor rather than a differently-verified one. And a whole-vault mode wants *more* sockets populated. `NP-COST-001` §6 evaluates only the BOM axis. **Still a principal decision, and `OI-COST-10` makes it a precondition on setting retail price.**
  - **(b) A whole-vault low-irradiance mode is affordable and unspecified — `OI-PWR-07`.** ~30 W lights all 80 sockets at ~6 mW/cm², inside the existing 45–50 W envelope, and Grade A Alzheimer's specifies *whole-head* geometry. **But 7.2 J/cm² in a 20-min session is below the ≥10 J/cm² threshold** — it is coverage, not a therapeutic dose, and must never be presented as one. Product + Clinical decision on whether it is a feature at all.
  - **(c) `OI-HEXTILE-21` — the 1064 nm channel is 9× below its own flagship protocol.** §4.3.2 gives CH_C **28 mW/cm²**; Grade A cognitive enhancement specifies **0.25 W/cm²**. A 90-site 1064-only tile still reaches only ~85 mW/cm². **η_wp ≈ 4.8 % emitter wall — more watts and more sites cannot close it.** Options: better emitter (extends `OI-HEXTILE-02` to CH_C), accept CW/long sessions, or **claim 1064 nm against the AD protocol (0.1–0.3 W/cm²) rather than the cognitive one**. **Do not make comparative 1064 nm claims until this resolves.**
  See `docs/np_pwr_budget_001.md` §3.4–§3.7, `docs/np_hw_hextile_001.md` §6.4/§9.3/§11, `docs/reference/competitive-position.md`.

Resolve with `NP-FW-PBM1064-001` as R-5's owner.

Per-tile drive is an input to the check, not a constant. Nothing in the delivered v2 wire format bounds this today, and `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §3.5 puts the unbounded worst case at **~2.0 kW**. `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` **SH2-DRC-02b**'s pass condition reads *"global governor present"* with no unit and needs one.
