Related: NP-HW-TCAP-001 (the cap wiring spec that would pin the electrode-to-channel map) still requires authoring, and the BOM delta for the 5 additional current sources is still uncosted — both already noted on the 2026-08-05 entry in `completed-decisions.md`. **Closed the same day it was raised.** `np_mod_clin_tacs_params_t` gained a third mask byte, `channel_mask_ext` (channels 16–20 in bits 0–4; bits 5–7 reserved and written clear), taking the struct from 7 to 8 bytes and moving `waveform` from offset 6 to 7; `encodeClinicalTacs()` emits all three mask bytes and the `size: 7` assertion in `hubCompiler.test.ts` is now 8. The UI bounds come from a new `clinicalTacsMaxChannels` / `CLINICAL_TACS_MAX_CHANNELS` = 21 in each platform's hardware-limits table rather than a literal, and the iOS `Stepper` goes to `2...21, step: 1` because 21 is odd. **The "nothing validates `channel_count`" half was closed too, and is the more consequential half:** all three validators now range-check 1–21 and report out of range as a hardware error, so an over-range count is refused rather than silently reduced; the encoder still clamps, but only as a backstop behind that check. `np_protocol_tests.c` is the one host target that includes both `np_hub_types.h` and `np_hd_config.h`, so it now asserts `NP_CLIN_TACS_CHANNELS == NP_HD_DRIVER_CHANNELS` along with the 8-byte size and every field offset. **The two items this entry listed as related did NOT close with it** — NP-HW-TCAP-001 is still unauthored and the 5-current-source BOM delta is still uncosted; both are now tracked as `OI-TACS-02` below, and the wire format committing to 21 channels makes them load-bearing rather than aspirational. Full record in `completed-decisions.md`.
- [x] **OI-DOC-01: NP-FW-HUB-001 was registered and cited as governing, but the spec was never written — RESOLVED 2026-09-13 (NP-FW-HUB-001 Rev 1, Issue #339)** — **Resolution (1) was taken**: the specification is authored against the shipped `firmware/hub_control/` behaviour, back-dating nothing, as a new design output. `docs/np_fw_hub_001.md` now exists at Rev 1 dated 2026-09-13 — not 2026-05-16, because the register's original date recorded a document that did not exist and a fabricated history is not a design record. **24 requirements met by the code, 4 explicitly NOT met and carried as open items (§10.2), 23 decisions, 10 risk rows, 12 design-review checks, 6 open items.** Option (2) — retire the number and re-scope under NP-SW-001 — was not taken: 48 citations resolve against the sections as written, and re-pointing them would have moved the cost rather than paid it. **The cost the item was tracking turned out to be concrete, and writing the document is what surfaced it. `OI-FWHUB-01`, blocking: transcranial PBM — CLAUDE.md §3 modality ① — has no dispatchable path at all.** `np_mod_pbm_*` is reachable only through slots 0–4, which are the retired zone slots `np_protocol_verify_and_parse()` rejects (`slot_id < NP_HUB_SLOT_FIRST_VALID`); and the replacement addressing, `NP_PROTO_TARGET_SOCKET_MASK`, parses and resolves through `np_module_map` and is then dropped by `dispatch_command()`, which handles no target kind but `NP_PROTO_TARGET_SLOT`. Each half is deliberate, correct and fail-closed — falling back to the slot path would deliver an eleven-socket frontal-left command to whatever sits in slot 0, and a wrong-site dose is not recoverable where a missed one is — and each is individually recorded (the parser rejection in `np_hub_config.h`; the drop as `OI-HUB-SOCKET-01`). **Their conjunction was recorded nowhere, and it is that no transcranial PBM protocol can execute on the shipped firmware.** Intranasal PBM (slot 9) is unaffected. What is missing is the socket-indexed dispatch registry; its hardware half is already proposed in `NP-HW-HEXTILE-001` §8.4 and `NP-HW-HUB-001` Rev 4 §7.2.2. **Two further findings.** Three source files (`np_mod_t2_stubs.c`, `np_adaptation_log.c/.h`) cited `NP-FW-HUB-001 Rev 2` against a document with no Rev 1 — re-pointed to Rev 1 §8.9 and §6.4 in the same change. And `NP_HUB_PROTO_VERSION` has reached 3 (v2 replaced the five-bit `slot_mask` with `slot_id` plus a target block; v3 grew `np_mod_tdcs_params_t` by `OI-CHARGE-04`'s declared electrode area) while the register still described Rev 1 — **a register entry naming a document that cannot be read cannot go visibly stale**, which is the property that made this absence expensive rather than merely untidy. `hubCompiler.ts` and `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` banners updated; §4 is now the wire format of record, and that no mechanical check enforces its agreement with the compiler is raised as `OI-FWHUB-03` against `NP-CONV-001` §8. **Writing the specification also found and fixed two live defects in `np_hub_control_app_main()`.** `OI-FWHUB-07`: `np_mod_reg_scan()` ran before `np_log_init()`, so every boot-time SHDR zone-auth record was stamped with a device session count of 0 and then discarded when the logger zeroed its buffer — boot-time module authentication reached SHDR not at all, for two independent reasons, under a source comment three lines away asserting that the log files open *"before the session logger writes any record"*. Records-integrity only, no emission path, and near-inert today because the zone slots those records describe are themselves retired — but it would have bitten the moment the callback follows the socket lattice. `OI-FWHUB-08`: the banner and the register both said "four tasks" where the code creates five. **Both are gated, and the reason is the same one that made `OI-DOC-01` expensive: nothing was reading it.** `np_hub_control_app_main()` is ARM-cross-only — `firmware/hub_control/src/` is in `HUB_SOURCES`, compiled under arm-none-eabi and in no host test — so no unit test could reach the sequence and none ever did. `scripts/check-hub-bringup-order.ts` now asserts four ordering constraints and counts the banner's task set against `xTaskCreate()`, refusing rather than passing vacuously when a named call is absent, and it was falsified **against the pre-fix commit**, where it reports exactly those two violations (`NP-CONV-001` §8). Wired into `tooling-ci.yml:hub-bringup-order`. **Not fully discharged:** the document is unapproved (`Approved By` blank, `OI-FWHUB-06`), so `21 CFR §820.30(d)`'s review-and-approve limb is outstanding even though the documentation limb is met; `docs/status/document-register.md` says so rather than implying a completed review. **Original item text retained below for the record.**
  - *(2026-08-05, as raised)* **OI-DOC-01: NP-FW-HUB-001 is registered and cited as governing, but the spec was never written (raised 2026-08-05, OPEN — decision owner: Design Authority)** — `docs/np_fw_hub_001.md` does not exist. NP-FW-HUB-001 Rev 1 is nonetheless registered in `docs/status/document-register.md` as the Hub Control Program specification, cited as the governing document at the top of `firmware/CMakeLists.txt` and `app/web/src/lib/hubCompiler.ts`, and referenced from ~48 files across `firmware/hub_control/`. The firmware is therefore its own only description, and `hubCompiler.ts` — which must produce a wire format the hub accepts — has no specification to compile against, only the C it is trying to match. **This is a design-control gap, not a code defect:** 21 CFR §820.30(d) expects design outputs to be documented such that conformance to design input can be verified, and a 510(k) reviewer following the register lands on nothing. Its register entry was additionally stale in two ways, both corrected 2026-08-05: it described the module registry as probing "all 11 zone + accessory slots" (retired — the lattice is 80 sockets addressed by UID auto-inventory via `np_module_map`) and as driving "zone_announce for bone conduction" (retired — the bone-conduction announce tone was removed per NP-HFE-002 Rev 1 §7.2). **Resolution options:** (1) author NP-FW-HUB-001 Rev 1 properly against the shipped `firmware/hub_control/` behaviour, back-dating nothing and treating it as a new design output; (2) formally retire the document number and re-scope the hub control program under NP-SW-001, updating the ~48 citations. Option (1) is expected but is genuine specification work, not a documentation tidy — deliberately **not** attempted as part of the 2026-08-05 consistency pass, which recorded the absence honestly instead of writing a placeholder.

- [x] **`OI-NPPS-MOBILE-01`: neither mobile NPPS parser implemented `zone` or `condition` top-level blocks — RESOLVED 2026-08-23 (NP-NPPS-REF-001 Rev 10)** — Both mobile parsers now implement §8 `zone` and §9 `condition` blocks, build the §1.6 single namespace, and validate a protocol's zone and condition references against it; the shipped library resolves with zero unresolved references. Resolution (1) was taken — implement the blocks — rather than (2), declaring zone expansion a hub-side concern, because the parsers could then never validate a reference and §1.6's loader guarantee would have become web-only. Four further gaps were found and closed in the same change: neither parser read a protocol's `conditions:`/`references:`; **Android's PBM targeting was still the retired five-slot `ZoneSelection` model** and could not express a named zone; `scripts/sync-conditions.ts` no longer transcribes the registry into Swift and Kotlin (Windows keeps its table — it has no NPPS parser); and §8's socket range was documented as `1..30` against a lattice of 80. Android `:core` verified green at 172 tests; the iOS half is unbuilt for want of an Xcode toolchain in the authoring environment.

- [ ] **`OI-TACS-02`: the 21-channel tACS driver has no hardware source of truth and no costed BOM — half (i) CLOSED 2026-09-09, half (ii) ANSWERED AND RE-SCOPED, item still OPEN (raised 2026-09-07 — decision owner: EE Lead / Design Authority)** — The successor to `OI-TACS-01`, which closed the *software* half on 2026-09-07: `np_mod_clin_tacs_params_t` gained `channel_mask_ext` (channels 16–20), every encoder and validator now works in 1–21, and NP-NPPS-REF-001 Rev 15 documents it. Two things the 2026-08-05 decision ("T2 clinical tACS driver: 21 channels, one per cap electrode") left open were carried on that entry as notes; closing OI-TACS-01 makes them load-bearing rather than aspirational, because the wire format now commits to 21 channels the hardware has never been specified to have. **(i) NP-HW-TCAP-001 does not exist.** The T2 cap wiring spec that would pin the electrode-to-channel map has never been authored, so `k_driver_channel[]` in `firmware/sloreta_hdtdcs/src/np_hd_montage.c` remains the interim authority — a firmware table standing in for a controlled hardware document. Every 4×1 ring selection and now every `channel_count` above 16 resolves through it. **(ii) The BOM delta is uncosted.** Going from 16 to 21 channels is 5 additional precision current sources plus their sense paths; that delta has never been costed against the T2 BOM, and per CLAUDE.md §2.1 every figure there is a floor that already excludes term U. Pro Entry and Pro Full are the two configurations carrying positive margin, so this is the one place a cost surprise is absorbed rather than compounding an existing negative. **Not a safety issue and not a regression:** the T2 module drivers are still stubs (`np_mod_t2_stubs.c`), so nothing decodes the struct today, and the safety MCU's own limit is unrelated — `NP_SAFETY_MAX_CHANNELS` (14) counts `NP_SAFETY_EN_*` enable lines, and the whole clinical-stim driver is one of them (`NP_SAFETY_CH_CLIN_STIM`), not 21. What closing this item needs is NP-HW-TCAP-001 authored and released, and the 5-channel BOM delta priced. **Half (i) is closed (2026-09-09): `NP-HW-TCAP-001` Rev 1 exists.** §3 specifies the electrode-to-driver-channel map as the identity map (`REQ-TCAP-02`), so `k_driver_channel[]` implements a controlled document rather than being the authority for one; `REQ-TCAP-03` keeps the ~60 mm non-aliasing rule live rather than historical; and `scripts/check-tcap-map.ts` diffs the document against the firmware tables and both channel-count constants in CI, falsified on ten injected perturbations first (`NP-CONV-001` §8). The document is DRAFT and deliberately scoped to the **wiring** — cap mechanics, sizing and connector are not specified and are `OI-TCAP-05`, and the cap turns out to be absent from `NP-ART-001` §2's artifact register entirely (`OI-TCAP-06`, a miss against `OI-ART-06`). **Half (ii) is answered but not as asked, and the item is under-scoped.** No silicon has ever been named, so no per-channel price exists to multiply — the 2026-08-05 decision's own wording is *"no BOM line, and no named silicon"* — and `NP-HW-TCAP-001` §7 therefore bounds it rather than pricing it: the **materiality threshold is $305/channel** (Pro Entry binds, at 5 × 1.639 against +$2,499/unit) against a **record-derived ceiling of ≤$26.75/channel** (Pro Entry's $833 old BOM less Home Standard's $405 leaves $428 for every T2 hardware addition together, all credited to the driver). A factor of 11: **the delta cannot flip either Pro row.** Stated falsifiably — selection above $305/channel reopens it. **But §7.3 finds the half scoped to the wrong term, in the same shape as `OI-COST-07` against `OI-HUB-C08`:** if the 16-channel baseline never had a BOM line either, the uncosted quantity is the whole 21-channel stage, not the 5-channel delta — and the conclusion survives on the **threshold** alone (21 channels at $305 is $6,403, or 2.9–3.0× the entire Pro Full BOM floor), not on the $26.75 ceiling, which located the driver inside the $428 envelope this premise denies. **And §7.4 names a larger uncosted term next door that no costing owns** — `OI-EEGNET-07` decides whether the T2 cap tail is 23 conductors or 44 (`NP-HW-EEGNET-001` §6.4: two conductors per dual-rated site if concurrent TMS and full 21-channel tES is required), a doubling of a per-unit harness for which `NP-COST-001` §2 A-1 has no row at all. Recorded as `NP-COST-001` Rev 3 §5.1. **What now remains for this item:** name the driver silicon (`OI-TCAP-03`) so the bound can become a price, and re-scope the cost half to the 21-channel stage (`OI-TCAP-04`). **A finding surfaced on the way that is more consequential than either half:** specifying what the cap conductor must carry required knowing whether 4 mA into a 3.5 mm electrode is permissible, and it is not answerable — `NP_HD_MAX_CURRENT_UA` (2 mA), `NP_HD_MAX_ELECTRODE_DENSITY_A_M2` (6.0 A/m²) and the 40 µC/cm² per-phase limit are **mutually unsatisfiable on a 0.0962 cm² electrode** (2 mA is 34.6× the density limit; the 4 mA tACS ceiling exceeds the charge limit 660× at 0.5 Hz and 3.3× at 100 Hz, because charge density is frequency-dependent and no constant expresses that), and the density constant has **no production consumer** — its only reference in the tree asserts the value of the `#define` itself. **Not a live hazard** (T2 drivers are stubs, no cap exists, the Class C interlock is independent) and deliberately not resolved: the three resolutions are a cap redesign, a capability reduction and a safety-basis change. `OI-TCAP-01` / `OI-TCAP-02`, EE Lead + Clinical, and it must not reach a cap drawing. **UPDATE 2026-09-20 (GitHub #332): the half the item's title names first — *no hardware source of truth* — is supplied.** `NP-HW-TACSDRV-001` Rev 1 states the driver's envelope (4 mA per channel **concurrent on all 21**, 0.5–100 Hz arbitrary waveform, against HD-tDCS's ≤ 2 mA DC at ≤ 5-of-21 concurrency), records that channel independence means **waveform** independence, restates that **one Class C bit gates all 21 channels** with any per-channel disable being a Class B availability gate (`OI-HUB-C07`'s precedent), and registers the stage as artifact **A16** in `NP-ART-001` §2.2 — the second artifact found missing from that register by following a citation, after the cap. **It names no silicon and sets no price**, deliberately. **It also finds a missing precondition for `OI-TCAP-03`:** no compliance voltage exists anywhere in the document set, and neither does a T2 cap electrode impedance — one decision, raised as **`OI-TACSDRV-02`**, and it must be answered *before* silicon can be chosen rather than after. **`OI-TACSDRV-01` (siting)** is the other new item and it is the one to sequence first: own T2 board, Hub PCB Rev C, or the posterior aggregation node — it decides which artifact row and which risk register own the stage, and the third option inherits `OI-HUB-C16`'s unresolved **tES current rating through the analog crosspoint**. **What remains for `OI-TACS-02` is unchanged in substance:** `OI-TCAP-03` (silicon, now against a stated envelope), `OI-TACSDRV-02`, `OI-TACSDRV-01` and `OI-TCAP-04` (cost half re-scoped). **`NP-HW-TCAP-001` is Rev 2** — a pointer revision only, no requirement or figure changed. **UPDATE 2026-09-23: `OI-TACSDRV-01` is analysed and recommended, not decided (`NP-HW-TACSDRV-001` Rev 2 §4.1.1).** The three candidates were two questions, an artifact boundary and a location. The recommendation: an **own T2-only board** (A16 keeps its row), with **sources and sense paths at the PAN**, where `REQ-NET-16` already lands the cap tail, so 21 in-band drive lanes never cross the parting-plane boss. **Compliance-supply magnetics go on the Hub PCB** per `OI-HUB-C19`, or there is no new stage if `OI-TACSDRV-02` allows an existing rail. Rev 1's crosspoint objection **does not attach**, because the T2 cap bypasses N4. The recommendation's falsifier is heat. The PAN has no thermal budget, and worst-case dissipation is about V_c × 84 mA, ~3.4 W at the 40 V that `NP-HW-TCAP-001` §4.3's 10 kΩ abort threshold implies. That is **`OI-TACSDRV-04`**, time-boxed to MECH-1's boss tooling. **`OI-TACSDRV-05`** records that SHELL-002 §3.5 still sizes N4 for T2 electrodes the cap tail no longer routes through it. **`OI-TACSDRV-06` (Product + Regulatory + ME): the T1 → T2 upgrade path is assumed but never specified.** Whether T2 is a new unit, a service conversion or user-installed parts decides what every T1 unit must be provisioned with, whether the hub-side supply survives, and the regulatory status of a converted wellness unit. `OI-TACSDRV-01` now depends on it. **UPDATE 2026-09-23 (later): `OI-TACSDRV-06` is analysed and recommended, not decided, in the new `NP-REG-UPG-001`.** The recommendation is **a new unit**: T2 is built as T2, and no T1 unit is converted by the depot, a partner or the user. Two T2 provisions cannot be added after manufacture. The PAN's ADS1299 bank is 8 channels on T1 and 21 on T2, on the laminated L1 carrier, and the TMS window is laid up into the never-opened outer bowl. Users already open the parting plane for tile swaps, so a conversion is distinguished by the factory-only assemblies it touches, not by opening the shell. **Under the recommendation, `OI-TACSDRV-01`'s Q1 holds with its saving in full and Q3's hub-side magnetics survive.** If the principal takes it, `OI-TACSDRV-01` waits only on the principal's call on the siting and on `OI-TACSDRV-04`. `NP-HW-TACSDRV-001` is Rev 3. The new open items are in the entry below.
- [x] **`OI-TACSDRV-06`: the T1 → T2 upgrade path — DECIDED 2026-09-23 by the principal: (a) new unit, with module carry-over (`NP-REG-UPG-001` Rev 2 §7.0; `NP-HW-TACSDRV-001` Rev 4)** — **No T1 unit is converted by the depot, a partner or the user, and none is upgraded piecemeal. The modules a purchaser bought carry over to the T2 they buy.** The principal's reasons: module reuse; avoiding the regulatory issues of conversion and user installation; and stopping a rolling upgrade that would get round the T1 → T2 price differential. All five points of `NP-REG-UPG-001` §7.1 were taken as written. **Applied in §7.4, in twelve rows.** T1 carries no populated T2 content. The TMS window is on T2 layups only (`OI-UPG-05`). No service tier changes a unit's tier. **Three requirements are derived in §7.5:** `REQ-UPG-01` (a T1-identity unit never enables a T2 modality or unlocks a T2 feature), `REQ-UPG-02` (the tier identity is written once, at manufacture) and `REQ-UPG-03` (every T1 module interface is kept on every T2). **Neither gate is implemented.** `OI-TACSDRV-01`'s dependency on this item is discharged. - [ ] **The power envelope against a specified rejection path — every thread in the #336 group terminates in heat, and the TMS coil gets its first electrical specification (NP-PWR-THERM-001 Rev 1, 2026-09-15, GitHub Issue #336)** — Takes `NP-THERM-SINK-001`'s `SPEC-SINK-01` as an input and re-derives what depends on it. Closes `OI-PWR-01`, `OI-SINK-02`, `OI-SINK-05`, `OI-POE-09` and `OI-PWR-11`; the residue is nine items, of which two are BLOCKING.
  - **(a) `OI-PWRTH-01` — BLOCKING. The TMS coil has no thermal path, and this is the whole finding at T2.** `SPEC-TMS` puts **33 W of winding dissipation** into a head-worn coil during a 10 Hz train (Q 69 → 9.2 % of stored energy per cycle → **3.30 J/pulse**). The adiabatic headroom of a 200 g assembly from 25 °C to the **same 42 °C applied-part limit the PBM tiles are held to** is 1,700 J, i.e. **516 pulses against a 3,000-pulse protocol (5.8× over)**, and **1.2× over for a single iTBS block**. No cooling path is specified anywhere, and CLAUDE.md §4.3 requires the window beneath the coil to be **non-conductive**, which forecloses the obvious metallic spreader — the same clearance problem `SPEC-SINK-04`'s graphite film has on the vault. **Given a number, the supply was the easy half** (176 W is an ordinary 240 W EPR contract); the heat is not. Specify a cooling path, a duty-cycle-aware thermal model, or a firmware pulse-count/train-gap governor — the last being `NP-PWRSRC-001` §5.5's *time-at-ceiling* problem arriving at T2.
  - **(b) `OI-PWR-02` — re-marked BLOCKING, and it now gates every T2 power, thermal and mechanical decision.** `NP-PWR-BUDGET-001` §4.2's concern is **confirmed, for a reason §4.2 did not give**. Its comparison of 0.1–0.5 T against a clinical figure-8's ~1.5–2.0 T is not like-for-like in either direction: the induced field goes as **B·ω** and this coil rings in 142 µs against ~250 µs, so it converts **1.8× more of each tesla** into induced field (a factor §4.2 charges it for and it does not owe); but a **25 mm wing falls off far faster with depth than a 45 mm one** — 0.48 against 0.76 at 20 mm — which §4.2 does not charge it for at all, and which is the larger of the two. With both credited, **0.5 T reaches 17 % of the reference coil's maximum cortical field and is 4.1× short of 120 % RMT**. Closing it by field alone needs **2.06 T → 613 J/pulse → 2,886 W**, which no PD class, mains accessory or head-worn thermal path in the programme reaches; the other lever is a larger wing, which trades back against the ring period. **Consequence: the TMS supply question has two answers 17× apart, and which is right is not a power question.** §9's `T2 + TMS` row is written at the lower one (**~176 W, 48 V/5 A, 240 W EPR**) and **labelled**, never averaged.
  - **(c) `OI-PWRTH-06` — CLAUDE.md §4.5 (LOCKED) needs a thermal-bound column and a T2 + TMS row.** Not edited by the study. **The T1-peak row is an electrical peak the assembly cannot spend**: 38.4 W of device draw at the fully-distributed thermal ceiling and 15.9 W at an authored N = 6 montage, against ~45–50 W. A pointer note has been added to `docs/reference/hardware-detail.md` §4.5 so no figure is quoted from the table bare; the table edit itself is a principal decision. This is `NP-PWRSRC-001` §13's proposal, now with the TMS row it could not supply.
  - **(d) `OI-PWRTH-03` — §2.2's second Pro Full brick: spare, or the 1170 nm applicator's supply?** Reading 2 (a dual-inlet TMS architecture) is **refuted** at 130 W against 176 W. The residual is commercial, not electrical. Route with `OI-PWRSRC-15`. **§2.2 itself requires no change from the specified thermal ceiling** (PWRTH-D-3): a ladder keyed to *peak draw* and a governor denominated in *sustained watts* are different quantities that were never in conflict — the appearance of conflict came from §4.5 carrying only one of them.
  - **(e) `OI-PWRTH-07` — is the TMS applicator passive or active?** §8.4's 33 W and `SPEC-TMS-02`'s 28 µF / 1,600 V bank push toward siting the driver in the applicator; head-worn mass pushes back. **This decides PWRTH-D-7's (d) — a PD inlet on the TMS driver — against its (a) fallback, a single 240 W EPR rail.**
  - **(f) `OI-PWRTH-08` — #335's governor: the form is decidable today, the constants are not.** `P_thermal(N, ambient, spreader)` is the quantity `OI-PWRSRC-12` asks for, with three properties it must carry rather than flatten: it **rises** with N (a tile-count governor gets the sign wrong; a watts governor ignoring N gets the magnitude wrong by 12× between N = 1 and N = 80), it is **spreader-conditional** per `SINK-D-1`, and it is **ambient-dependent** in a way `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2's flat +35 °C gate cannot see (`OI-N1-08`). Its constants are blocked on `OI-SINK-01` and `OI-HEXTILE-02`, both of which enter multiplicatively.
  - **(g) `OI-PWRTH-05` — the active-shielding claim is unqualified for TMS, and this falls out of CLAUDE.md §4.2 alone.** The interlock gates Helmholtz cancellation off 5 ms before each pulse and holds it 50 ms — **55 ms per pulse**, so **55 % of a 10 Hz train, continuous above 18.2 Hz, and the whole of every 50 Hz iTBS burst.** Not a safety finding: the gate exists because the coils must not fight the pulse. A **claim** finding: CLAUDE.md §1's *"only consumer brain wearable with measured shielding"* has its active half off for most of a TMS train (the passive stack is unaffected), and nothing in the document set says so.
  - **(h) Two smaller residuals.** `OI-PWRTH-02` — `NP-PWRSRC-001` §7.0's *"2/23 under every candidate source"* is now **spreader-conditional** and must not be quoted bare (§7.0's *conclusion* survives; no source changes coverage). `OI-PWRTH-04` — no marketing or positioning copy may read §2.2's charger ladder as a **capability** ladder: at T1 the 65 W rung is above the thermal ceiling twice over, so "more watts, more protocols" is false. `OI-PWRTH-09` — `SPEC-TMS-01`'s coil geometry is chosen by the study, not specified by ME; wing radius is the single largest lever and sets depth falloff, L, the ring period and the energy at once, so a returned envelope re-runs everything downstream through one script. Co-decide with (b); #332 records that artifact A15 has no owning document either.
  **Nine decisions `PWRTH-D-1…9` (six to principal), five risk rows, nine open items `OI-PWRTH-01…09` (one BLOCKING). No locked section modified; no firmware, app or protocol changed; no figure in a released document rewritten.** Adds `scripts/check-power-envelope.ts`; adds `R_SINK_SPECIFIED` and `rSinkNote()` to `scripts/check-thermal-multitile.ts`. See `docs/np_pwr_therm_001.md`.

- [ ] **The EMF shielding claim had no evidence record, and a per-layer value audit finds one layer failing and the whole audit unrunnable (NP-BIB-EMF-001 Rev 1, 2026-09-15)** — CLAUDE.md §1 calls the 5-layer stack the product's primary technical differentiator and `competitive-position.md` carries published copy for it, yet it had **zero mentions in `clinical.md`, `regulatory-strategy.md`, `marketing-notes.md` or `np_cost_001.md`**, and is absent from the 33-entry FTC substantiation bibliography. `docs/np_bib_emf_001.md` closes the bibliography half. It asserts **no measurement** — `EMF-1`, `EMF-3`, `RISK-20` and `OI-THCOOL-06` are unchanged and open. Ten open items `OI-BIBEMF-01…10`, three gating. Five things for the principal:
  - **(a) `OI-BIBEMF-07` — the standing cost-justification principle cannot be applied to this stack, because the stack is uncosted.** `NP-COST-001` carries **no BOM line for any part of it** — not mu-metal, palladium, absorber foam, port filters, fluxgates or Helmholtz coils. The only figure in the cost record is `durability-maintenance.md` §30's **+$6/headset palladium-over-silver delta**, which is a choice between two options and not the cost of a layer. CLAUDE.md §2.1 already warns every cost figure is a floor excluding term **U**; this is a *second* uncosted term and unlike U it is flagged nowhere. Against four gross-margin-negative T1 configurations, **this gates the honest version of the audit.**
  - **(b) ~~`OI-BIBEMF-08`~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-09-20 by `NP-EMC-CAV-001`. Layer 4's requirement is stated at last, and the layer supplies 1 % of it.** The burden Rev 1 placed on EMC has been answered, and **neither** branch it offered applies as written. **The source is named and it is not a radio:** `NP-DRV-SHELL-002` §3.2 puts **18 STM32G071 cluster controllers** inside the envelope with a 400 kHz I2C tree, 80 PWM LED drivers and the ADS1299 SPI bank, and §9.6 of that document already said *"a Faraday cage does not protect the EEG electrodes and fluxgates that share the enclosure with the source"* — the answer was one document away, and Rev 1's own caveat pointed at it. **The band is 420 MHz – 3 GHz**, and its lower edge is a property of the wearer: 506 MHz at a 52 cm head, 422 MHz at 62 cm, so one SKU sweeps 84 MHz of resonance across the population — recorded nowhere before. **The requirement is stated:** `REQ-CAV-01` E ≤ 0.5 V/m (from `SH2-DRC-16` via RF demodulation, which `REQ-EMI-05` **cannot** subtract) and `REQ-CAV-02` **Q_L ≤ 20, i.e. ≥ 26.2 dB**. **Layer 4 supplies 0.26 dB of it; the wearer's head supplies 49.8 dB.** The reason is structural: for a lossy slab on a conductor `Z_in = j·η·tan(kd)`, and in the thin limit `η·k = η₀·k₀` exactly, so `Z_in → j·η₀·k₀·d` is purely reactive **independent of the loading** — 3 mm is λ/217 at the lowest mode, and even at an absurd ε_r of 6 − j12 the layer reaches 2.71 dB. **See the new principal item below.**
  - **(c) Layer 2 cannot be removed, and the reason is not its documented one.** The evidence does **not** support its stated rationale: WHO residential ELF is **0.07–0.11 µT** against **±200 µT** for the only reported EEG effects and a **null** result at 1,800 µT — 2,000–18,000× — and mu-metal is MEG technology, EEG not requiring external magnetic-field suppression. It stays on three grounds none of which `hardware-detail.md` §4.3 states: `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3.1 **co-designs the Helmholtz coils with the mu-metal as one magnetic circuit** (removing it invalidates the coil-drive→field transfer the whole active loop is calibrated against, plus `REQ-EMI-05` and `REQ-EMI-11`); `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2 makes it the **degraded-mode fallback** that keeps the active-cancellation envelope SOFT; and **`OI-BIBEMF-04`** — **IEC 60601-1-2 is missing from `regulatory-strategy.md` §8's required-standards list** while its IEC 61000-4-8 magnetic-immunity clause (30 A/m ≈ 37.7 µT) may be what requires the layer. **That item gates any L2 removal.**
  - **(d) `OI-BIBEMF-10` — the stack is aperture-limited, not layer-limited, which reprices all marginal shielding spend.** Every open item against the claim is a **seam or a hole**: `RISK-20` (rim Ra → slot leakage, BLOCKING for tooling), `EMF-3` (gasket line pressure), `OI-THCOOL-06` (boss penetration — *"a hole in mu-metal is a hole"*, BLOCKING), `EMF-1`, `OI-PWR-12`. The weakest aperture sets the floor and layers above it are wasted — which `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.2 reached independently when it consolidated everything onto one unbroken bowl. **Marginal spend belongs on seam control, not layer count, and `EMF-1` is not merely verification — it is the measurement that reveals which layers contribute at all.**
  - **(e) Two claim findings and one new risk.** `OI-BIBEMF-01` — *"Only consumer brain wearable with measured shielding"* has **both** load-bearing words unearned: *"measured"* (`EMF-1` never ran) and *"only consumer"* (research-grade dry-EEG headsets ship Faraday shielding today), so it **gates the `competitive-position.md` copy line**. `OI-BIBEMF-09` — the **+$6/headset palladium premium is justified in the record by a marketing rationale only** (*"marketable, measurable claim"*); whether silver tarnish measurably degrades RF shielding effectiveness at 0.8–6 GHz has never been asked, and if it does not, the premium buys a claim rather than a capability — the same error `competitive-position.md` §34 identified when it retired the LED-count claim. `OI-BIBEMF-06` — **no degaussing or remanence-drift procedure exists** for the mu-metal liner, while impact events (already logged in SHDR) can re-magnetise it and `REQ-EMI-11` recalibrates on `np_module_map` rebuild, which an impact is not.
  **The question behind the question (`OI-BIBEMF-05`):** L2, the fluxgates, the Helmholtz coils and `REQ-EMI-10` stand or fall **together**, and together they gate `SPEC-SINK-04`'s spreader against a BLOCKING `OI-SINK-01` with 4 of 22 protocols waiting. The decision worth taking is not *"remove a layer"* but **"is the active-cancellation subsystem earning its architectural cost?"** — a §4.3 locked-decision reopening, with `NP-THERM-SINK-001`/`NP-PWR-THERM-001` as the cost side and `NP-BIB-EMF-001` §4 as the benefit side. **No locked section modified; no layer removed; no measurement asserted.** See `docs/np_bib_emf_001.md`.

- [x] **✅ DECIDED 2026-09-23 — `REQ-CAV-04` TAKEN (principal, GitHub #391): Layer 4 is deleted and the 3 mm re-loft binds; executed across the set (CLAUDE.md Rev 51, `NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 14, §8.4's list). Follow-ons stay open and none gates it: `OI-EMCCAV-01`, `-02`, `-03`, `-04`, `-06`, `-07`, `-08` (now the station's only return path), `-11`, `-12`, `-14`; thermal re-baseline at 0.335 is `OI-THCOOL-21` (**re-baselined 2026-09-23, GitHub #407 — see `OI-SINK-10` below**); the shell re-loft rides `OI-ART-01` / #331.** PRINCIPAL DECISION — delete the Layer 4 station, with the 3 mm bowl re-loft binding (`NP-EMC-CAV-001` Rev 3, 2026-09-20, GitHub #362 / PR #365) — `OI-BIBEMF-08` asked EMC to state Layer 4's requirement in dB against a named source and band, or lose the layer. **It is stated** (`REQ-CAV-02`: loaded Q ≤ 20 over 420 MHz – 3 GHz, i.e. **≥ 26.2 dB**), and **Layer 4 supplies 0.26 dB while the wearer's head supplies 49.8 dB**. The recommendation is **delete**, and the one condition attached to it is mechanical, not electrical.
  - **(a) The 3 mm re-loft of the outer bowl is BINDING, and this is the finding worth carrying.** Vacating a 3 mm station **does not delete its resistance — it fills it with stagnant air**, at **0.115 m²K/W against the foam's 0.075, 54 % worse per mm** (both conductivities from `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §2's own table). Outward path: today **0.410** · **delete without closing the gap 0.450** — *a regression* · **delete with the 3 mm re-loft 0.335** — the best figure available · ceramic-filled substitution 0.355. The re-loft and the deletion are **one change**, not two.
  - **(b) An intermediate revision argued for keeping the layer on two costs that do not exist, and both are worth naming so the decision is not re-litigated on them.** It called the re-loft *"a shell tooling change"* — but **no tooling is cut**: `NP-REV-SHELL-001` is *"DRAFT — open review; no item signed"*, the programme is **pre-tooling**, and `OI-ART-01` **already owes** a re-scope of `NP-TOOL-SHELL-001` (its F-01 still describes the retired 5-colour zone-slot scheme), so the re-loft rides along with work already due. And it called *"5-layer"* **a published claim** — but **nothing is externally published**; `competitive-position.md`'s own source note says its copy *"should be re-verified before publication"*, and the shielding line already carries a ⚠ that two of its words are unearned. **This is the cheapest moment this decision will ever be available.**
  - **(c) ~~`OI-EMCCAV-08` is the only open question~~ — ANSWERED by `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §8.3, and it no longer gates.** **The clamps lose no space to the re-loft.** The absorber sits at station L2 **outboard of the Gap** the clamps live in, so deleting it moves the Pd liner and everything outboard **3 mm inward together**: the **5–7 mm Gap is preserved**, the **3.0–4.0 mm of clamp features inside L1 is untouched**, and only the clamp's *counterface* changes — from compressible foam to **rigid Pd/mu-metal/CFRP**, which is what an over-center lever wants (a compliant counterface gives it a drifting over-center point as the foam takes compression set). **And the tolerance argument inverts:** the foam is a **±0.5 contributor**, so the clamp load path **shrinks from ±1.30 to ±0.80 worst-case, 38 %**. Residual, and `MECH-2`'s regardless: confirm the spring-plunger stroke covers ±0.80 — *easier than today's ±1.30*. **`OI-THCOOL-15` closes outright as a side effect** — *"what the pad compresses against"* ceases to exist once the pad presses on the rigid bowl. **New `OI-EMCCAV-09`:** the 5–7 mm Gap is itself an **allocation, not a derived requirement** — no throw or stroke figure exists anywhere. *(Superseded framing, retained: the question had been whether the clamps and plungers could still take up the stack without the foam.)* The same revision also claimed the foam is the **compliant member** taking up the ±0.5 tolerance stack. `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a says otherwise: the preload path is **over-center lever-throw cluster clamps with per-module spring plungers** (`MECH-2`). The foam is *incidentally* compressible — which is exactly why it obstructs `OI-THCOOL-15`'s pad — not a designed member. **The question to settle before cutting the re-loft into CAD:** once 3 mm of incidental compliance leaves the gap, do the clamps and plungers still take up the stack across the curved span? **If yes → delete and re-loft (0.335).** **If no → the station returns as a thin ceramic-filled pad sized by the tolerance stack** — not by the 3.0 mm an RF justification happened to pick — at ~0.355 (`REQ-CAV-03`, insulating and non-magnetic).
  - **(d) Why the residual 0.020 is not worth buying back.** Once ceramic-filled, the station's *bulk* resistance is ~0.002 m²K/W (3 mm at k ≈ 1.5). The 0.020 separating substitution from deletion is therefore almost entirely **contact resistance** — the price of pressing a compliant pad against two curved surfaces. Deletion removes the interface, not just the material.
  - **(e) What the deletion touches, correctly priced.** `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2/§70 (radial total 30–35 → **27–32 mm**) — CAD · `NP-TOOL-SHELL-001` — **none, the re-scope is already owed** · CLAUDE.md §1/§4.3, `hardware-detail.md` §4.3, `DI-PERF-22` (*"5-layer"* → 4-layer) — internal string edits · `competitive-position.md` §176 — a copy edit **before** first publication · `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §2 — the 18 % term leaves, which is the point · `scripts/check-section-refs.ts` — one CI run. **The renumbering caution rides with it:** `L5` must **not** silently become `L4`, or every existing citation of "Layer 4" quietly means port filters.
  - **(f) Two things measurement could still change, and one it cannot.** `EMF-1a`/`EMF-1b` gate **confidence, not the decision** — §6.1's result is structural, so they can only show the layer does *less* than 0.26 dB, never more. **`OI-EMCCAV-06` is the one place a measurement could change the answer**: the band's upper edge is derived against the **internal** source only, and says nothing about **external 6 GHz Wi-Fi ingress through the parting-plane seam** (the case `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.3a and `RISK-20` are written against), where the foam is **no longer electrically thin** — §6.2 gives it 11–41 dB there. **Sweep `EMF-1a` to 6 GHz, absorber fitted vs removed, before the re-loft is cut into CAD.** And `OI-EMCCAV-07` — nobody has budgeted the heat that moves into the **outer bowl** (mu-metal + Helmholtz) when outward resistance falls, against `NP-ENV-OPRANGE-001` §2's SOFT envelope and `REQ-EMI-11`'s transfer function; that applies to every option including deletion.
  - **(g) A LARGER lever than this decision surfaced while answering (f), and it is `MECH-2`'s + Thermal's, not the principal's today.** **`FLUSH-1`** (principal direction 2026-09-20, `NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.4a): the cluster lever is **flush when closed and throws only with the bowls separated** — which §5.2 already implied (*"reached by unclamping the bowls"*) without anyone drawing the consequence. So *"inter-bowl clamp **travel**"* in `NP-HELMET-GEOM-001` §2's **5–7 mm** Gap allocation is **not an assembled-state requirement**, and the gap can be re-dimensioned against the **closed** lever footprint, the labyrinth lip, the fluxgates and the boss — the boss being a **standalone posterior-centre feature**, so a **locally-relieved** gap is available. **The gap is stagnant air at 0.231 m²K/W — 56 % of the outward path and its single largest term — so each millimetre is worth 0.0385 m²K/W. Two millimetres beats deleting the whole Layer 4 absorber (0.077 vs 0.075); three recovers 0.115.** Against `NP-THERM-COOL-001` §6.1's sealed recirculation (recovery 0.164, **needs a motor in the sealed cavity**) that is **~70 % of the prize with no motor, no power and no BOM line** — though the two **interact rather than compose** and must be traded. **`OI-EMCCAV-09` re-scoped to carry the hand-off.** Nothing here changes the Layer 4 recommendation above; it changes what the *next* thermal decision should be about.
  **Nothing is deleted and no locked section is modified.** `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §8.2 states the recommendation and routes it here. See `docs/np_emc_cav_001.md`. **→ Superseded 2026-09-23: the principal took it; the station is deleted and CLAUDE.md §1 / §4.3 now read 4-layer.**

- [x] **✅ DECIDED 2026-09-24 (principal direction, PR #412) — YES: run as `EMF-1f` (`NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.6.1), on a second outer bowl at the pre-re-loft radius, liner fitted then removed, empty and head-fitted, 300 MHz – 6 GHz, with the liner's permittivity measured. The article (not a temporary liner in the re-lofted bowl) was chosen in writing the row, for the mode-shift reason below.** ~~PRINCIPAL DECISION — add a foam-fitted comparison run to the `EMF-1` test plan, or not?~~ (`NP-HEX-ZM-001` §5.6.1, raised 2026-09-23 by `OI-EMCCAV-04`, GitHub #401; bears on `OI-EMCCAV-06` / #402).** `NP-EMC-CAV-001` §8.2, written before `REQ-CAV-04` was taken, asked for `EMF-1a` at 6 GHz *"with the absorber fitted and removed"*. The plan now contains only the removed half, the as-designed stack. **For:** above 3 GHz the foam was no longer electrically thin (11–41 dB at 3 GHz, §6.2); that is the only region where the deleted layer did measurable work, and it is the region `OI-EMCCAV-06` asks about. **Against:** below 3 GHz it cannot change anything (§6.1 is structural). Above 3 GHz there is no requirement to judge the difference against, and whether the as-designed enclosure is adequate there is answered by the as-designed sweep. **Cost:** a test article outside the design. A temporary 3 mm liner in the re-lofted bowl takes 3 mm out of the 23–29 mm cavity and shifts the modes; the like-for-like option is a second outer bowl at the pre-re-loft radius. **Non-gating:** until decided, `EMF-1a`–`EMF-1d` run on the as-designed stack. §8.2 timed its request as *"before the re-loft is cut into CAD"* (`OI-ART-01` / #331), so a *yes* is cheapest before then.

Attribution (`NP-THERM-SINK-001` Rev 3 §3a): removing the foam alone moves the face **−0.02 K**; the smaller exterior alone **+0.19 K**. **Not a control gap** — the 42 °C interlock and Path B1's face NTC observe it exactly as before — and **no limit has been adjusted**. Options: **(a)** accept, recording that the deletion is thermally neutral-to-slightly-adverse at the scalp rather than favourable; **(b)** take the re-loft on the **inner face only**, holding the exterior where it was (a thicker outer bowl: mass, and a `MECH-2` / `NP-TOOL-SHELL-001` re-scope question — nothing is cut yet); **(c)** other. `THERM-1b` (`OI-R1-02`) is what would measure it.
